# polyctl

`polyctl` is a single command-line tool for the accounts most small teams end up juggling: cloud platforms (AWS, GCP, Alibaba Cloud), CDN/DNS (Cloudflare) and domain registrars (name.com, GoDaddy).

It does two things at once:

- **Platform-neutral commands** (`polyctl dns ...`, `polyctl domain ...`, `polyctl cert ...`) work on any domain regardless of where it lives. polyctl looks the domain up in a local index, finds the account that owns it and routes the call there. `polyctl find <ip|dns-name>` answers "whose IP is this?" across every account.
- **Platform-specific commands** (`polyctl cloudflare ...`, `polyctl aws ...`, ...) expose each platform's native features with that platform's own vocabulary. Nothing is flattened to a lowest common denominator.

Everything it learns about your accounts is cached in a local SQLite inventory, so reads are instant and work offline; writes always go to the platform and update the cache.

## Features

- Six platforms: Cloudflare, name.com, GoDaddy, AWS, GCP, Alibaba Cloud.
- Profiles that group accounts: one profile holds one account on each platform a project uses (`acme` = its Cloudflare, name.com and AWS accounts), and you can keep as many profiles as you have projects or customers.
- Secrets in the OS keyring (macOS Keychain, Windows Credential Manager, Secret Service on Linux) with an automatic fallback to a `0600` file; `config.yaml` never holds a secret.
- Reverse lookup: `polyctl find 203.0.113.10` lists the instances, load balancers, elastic / static IPs and database endpoints that hold an address or DNS name, in every account, with console links.
- Domain routing: `polyctl dns list example.com` finds the right zone and account by itself. Conflicts (the same domain in two accounts) are reported instead of guessed; `--profile` and `--platform` pick one.
- Certificates: `polyctl cert ensure` issues and renews Let's Encrypt certificates through a DNS-01 challenge in whichever account hosts the domain, and `polyctl cert deploy` puts them on load balancers and certificate stores (Alibaba Cloud ALB and CAS, GCP HTTPS proxies, AWS ACM).
- Cloud resources: instances (EC2, Compute Engine, ECS) with create, delete, start, stop, reboot and resize, security groups and firewalls, load balancers, EBS volumes and snapshots, RDS instances, key pairs, elastic IPs, VPCs, and platform certificates (ACM, CAS, GCP SSL certificates).
- Idempotent `ensure` commands (`dns ensure`, `cloudflare zone ensure`, `aws elbv2 load-balancer ensure`, `aliyun ecs instance ensure`, ...) that change only what differs and print a change report; `--dry-run` shows the report of any write command without writing, and every destructive command asks first (`--yes` in scripts).
- Local inventory (`inventory.db`): per-resource-type TTLs, `--refresh` to force a fetch, `--offline` to forbid the network, `polyctl sync` to bulk-load.
- `table` (default), `json` and `yaml` output for every command.
- Distinct exit codes for usage, auth, not-found, ambiguous, offline and not-supported errors.
- Shell completion for bash, zsh, fish and PowerShell, including profile, platform, domain-name and record-id completion from the local inventory.

## Install

Prebuilt binaries are published for macOS, Linux and Windows (x86-64 and ARM64). No Go toolchain is needed.

### Homebrew (macOS, Linux)

```sh
brew install --cask gofika/tap/polyctl
```

Shell completions for bash, zsh and fish are installed with it. Upgrade with `brew upgrade --cask polyctl`.

### Scoop (Windows)

```powershell
scoop bucket add gofika https://github.com/gofika/scoop-bucket
scoop install polyctl
```

Upgrade with `scoop update polyctl`.

### Install script

macOS and Linux:

```sh
curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/gofika/polyctl/main/install.sh | sh
```

Windows (PowerShell):

```powershell
irm https://raw.githubusercontent.com/gofika/polyctl/main/install.ps1 | iex
```

The scripts download the latest release for your platform, verify its SHA-256 checksum and install the binary into `~/.local/bin` (macOS, Linux) or `%LOCALAPPDATA%\polyctl\bin` (Windows, added to your user `PATH`). Run the same command again to upgrade.

| Variable | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `POLYCTL_VERSION` | Install a specific version (for example `0.1.2`; see the [releases](https://github.com/gofika/polyctl/releases)) instead of the latest release. |
| `POLYCTL_INSTALL_DIR` | Install the binary into this directory. |

```sh
curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/gofika/polyctl/main/install.sh | POLYCTL_VERSION=0.1.2 POLYCTL_INSTALL_DIR="$HOME/bin" sh
```

### Manual download

Download the archive for your platform from the [releases page](https://github.com/gofika/polyctl/releases), check it against `checksums.txt`, and put `polyctl` somewhere on your `PATH`.

### Build from source

For contributors; requires Go.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/gofika/polyctl.git
cd polyctl
make build        # writes bin/polyctl
```

### Shell completion

Homebrew installs completions automatically, and the release archives ship them under `completions/`. To generate them yourself:

```sh
# bash
polyctl completion bash > /etc/bash_completion.d/polyctl        # or: source <(polyctl completion bash)

# zsh
polyctl completion zsh > "${fpath[1]}/_polyctl"                 # then restart the shell

# fish
polyctl completion fish > ~/.config/fish/completions/polyctl.fish

# PowerShell
polyctl completion powershell | Out-String | Invoke-Expression
```

Completion is context-aware: `--profile`, `auth use` and the first argument of `auth rename` complete configured profiles, `auth login`, `auth logout` and `--platform` complete platform names, and the `<domain>` argument of `dns`, `domain` and `cert` commands completes from the local domain index.

## Quick start

```sh
# 1. Log in to the accounts you use (secrets go to the keyring).
#    With no profile current, the first login creates the profile "default"
#    and makes it current; the following ones add their platform to it.
polyctl auth login cloudflare
polyctl auth login namecom
polyctl auth login aws --set method=sso --set start_url=https://acme.awsapps.com/start --set sso_region=us-east-1

#    A second project gets its own profile
polyctl auth login cloudflare --profile acme
polyctl auth login godaddy    --profile acme

# 2. Check that everything works
polyctl auth status

# 3. Build the domain index (which account owns which domain / zone)
polyctl domain index

# 4. Use platform-neutral commands; routing is automatic
polyctl domain which example.com
polyctl dns list example.com
polyctl dns set example.com --type A --name www --value 203.0.113.10
polyctl dns ensure example.com -r www:A:203.0.113.10:300 -r @:MX:"10 mail.example.com"
polyctl domain ns set example.com ns1.cloudflare.com ns2.cloudflare.com

#    When several accounts know the domain, say which one you mean
polyctl dns list example.com --platform cloudflare
polyctl dns list example.com --profile acme --platform cloudflare

# 5. Or talk to one platform directly
polyctl aws ec2 instance list                 # the AWS account of the current profile
polyctl cloudflare zone list --profile acme   # the Cloudflare account of another profile
```

Status lines (`zone example.com via cloudflare/default`) go to **stderr**; results go to **stdout**, so `polyctl dns list example.com -o json | jq` works as expected.

## Configuration

All state lives in one directory, `~/.config/polyctl/` by default (created with mode `0700`):

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `config.yaml` | Profiles and settings. Metadata only, never secrets. Written atomically with mode `0600`. |
| `inventory.db` | SQLite cache of platform resources and the domain index, and the encrypted login credentials of the Windows instances polyctl creates (see Instance login credentials). |
| `credentials.json` | Secrets, **only** when the OS keyring is unavailable or `credential_store: file` is set. Mode `0600`. |
| `credentials.lock` | Empty lock file next to it. polyctl holds a lock on it while it changes stored secrets, so that several polyctl processes running at once do not drop each other's entries. Safe to delete when no polyctl is running. |

### Environment variables and global flags

| Variable | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `POLYCTL_CONFIG_DIR` | Use this directory instead of `~/.config/polyctl`: it holds `config.yaml`, the file credential store and `inventory.db`. `--config-dir` overrides it. |
| `POLYCTL_OUTPUT` | Output format (`table`, `json`, `yaml`). `--output` overrides it; it overrides the `output:` key in `config.yaml`. |
| `POLYCTL_CREDENTIAL_STORE` | Overrides `credential_store` in `config.yaml` (`auto`, `keyring`, `file`). |
| `POLYCTL_INVENTORY_DB` | Path of the SQLite inventory file (default `<config dir>/inventory.db`). |
| `POLYCTL_NON_INTERACTIVE` | `1`, `true` or `yes` forces the non-interactive flow, like `--non-interactive`: nothing is ever asked. `0`, `false`, `no` or empty leave it off; any other value is a usage error (exit code 2). See [Interactive and non-interactive use](#interactive-and-non-interactive-use). |

| Global flag | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `--config-dir <dir>` | The configuration directory: the one that holds `config.yaml`, the file credential store (`credentials.json`) and `inventory.db`. The exact counterpart of `POLYCTL_CONFIG_DIR`, which it overrides. Created with mode `0700` when missing. |
| `-p, --profile <name>` | Use this profile. Overrides the current profile and restricts automatic routing, `sync`, `domain index` and `auth status` to the accounts of this profile. For `auth login` it names the profile the account is added to. |
| `--refresh` | Ignore the cache: fetch from the platform and update the inventory. |
| `--offline` | Never touch the network; serve from the inventory only. Mutually exclusive with `--refresh`. |
| `-o, --output <fmt>` | `table` (default), `json` or `yaml`. Overrides `POLYCTL_OUTPUT` and `output:` in `config.yaml`. |
| `-v, --verbose` | Extra diagnostics on stderr. |
| `-y, --yes` | Assume "yes" for confirmations, in both flows: the commands listed in [Confirmations](#confirmations), the interactive `auth logout` and the removal inside `auth manage`. In the non-interactive flow it is the only way to confirm: without it such a command is a usage error (exit code 2). It confirms, it does not choose: a missing argument is still asked for on a terminal, and still a usage error without one. |
| `--dry-run` | Write commands report what they would change and write nothing: every command that writes to a platform honours it, prints the change report of [Dry runs and change reports](#dry-runs-and-change-reports) with `would create` / `would update` / `would delete`, and exits with code 0. Reads still happen (a dry run looks at the current state to tell what would change). Nothing is confirmed either: a dry run asks no question and needs no `--yes`, also together with `--yes`. Read-only commands ignore the flag. The commands that change local configuration only (`auth login`, `auth use`, `auth logout`, `auth rename`, `auth manage`) refuse it with a usage error rather than change anything. |
| `--non-interactive` | Force the non-interactive flow, also on a terminal: never ask, choose or confirm anything. Missing arguments and unconfirmed actions are usage errors (exit code 2) that name what to pass. Same as `POLYCTL_NON_INTERACTIVE=1`, which the flag overrides: `--non-interactive=false` keeps a terminal interactive although the variable is set. With `--yes`: no question at all, and destructive actions proceed. |
| `--version` | Print the build version. |

Every setting follows one rule of precedence: **command-line flag > environment variable > `config.yaml` > built-in default**. So `--config-dir` beats `POLYCTL_CONFIG_DIR`, `--output` beats `POLYCTL_OUTPUT` beats `output:`, `POLYCTL_CREDENTIAL_STORE` beats `credential_store:`, and `--non-interactive` beats `POLYCTL_NON_INTERACTIVE`: either one switches the non-interactive flow on, and `--non-interactive=false` leaves the choice to the terminal check even when the variable is set. (Nothing makes a command interactive without a terminal on stdin and stderr.)

Help never depends on a setting: `--help`, `polyctl help <command>`, bare `polyctl` and a bare command group (`polyctl dns`, `polyctl aws ec2`) print help and exit with code 0 without reading the configuration or creating the configuration directory. `polyctl help` followed by a word that names no command is a usage error (exit code 2), like the command itself.

Flags that only some commands have are local to them:

- `auth login` and `auth manage` take `--no-browser`: during a login, print URLs instead of opening a browser. It is implied in the non-interactive flow, which never opens one.
- The `dns` and `domain` command groups, `cert ensure` and `cert deploy` take `--platform <platform>`, which routes to the account on that one platform (and restricts any indexing to it); see [Routing rules](#routing-rules).
- `sync`, `inventory list` and `find` have a `--platform` of their own, a filter that takes a list of platforms (comma-separated); see [Caching](#caching) and [Finding resources by IP or DNS name](#finding-resources-by-ip-or-dns-name).

### `config.yaml`

```yaml
current_profile: acme             # the profile platform commands and `auth login` use by default
credential_store: auto            # auto (default) | keyring | file
output: table                     # table | json | yaml

ttl:                              # freshness window per resource type (Go durations)
  default: 24h
  dns_record: 1h
  zone: 24h
  domain: 24h
  instance: 15m

profiles:
  acme:                           # one profile, one account per platform
    platforms:
      cloudflare:
        metadata:
          account_id: 0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef
          email: ops@example.com
        credential_backend: keyring
        created_at: 2026-11-01T10:00:00Z
        updated_at: 2026-11-01T10:00:00Z
      namecom:
        metadata:
          username: acme
          env: production
        credential_backend: keyring
      aws:
        metadata:
          method: sso
          start_url: https://acme.awsapps.com/start
          sso_region: us-east-1
          account_id: "123456789012"
          role_name: AdministratorAccess
          region: eu-west-1
        credential_backend: keyring
  lab:
    platforms:
      aws:
        metadata:
          method: keys
          region: us-east-1
        credential_backend: keyring
```

Keys:

- `current_profile`: the profile a **platform-specific** command uses when you did not pass `--profile`, provided it has an account on that platform. If it has none, polyctl falls back to "the only profile with an account on that platform", and errors with the candidate list when there are several. It is also the profile `auth login` adds an account to when `--profile` is not given. Set by `auth use`, and by `auth login` when no profile is current. A value that names no profile (after a hand edit) is treated as not set: `auth login` then says so, uses `default` and makes that profile current.
- `credential_store`: `auto` tries the keyring and falls back to the file (printing a warning once); `keyring` fails if no keyring is available; `file` always uses `credentials.json`.
- `ttl`: how long a synced set stays fresh, per resource type (see [Caching](#caching) for the types). Unset types use `default`; the built-in defaults are `dns_record: 1h`, `zone: 24h`, `domain: 24h`, `instance: 15m`, `security_group: 1h`, `load_balancer: 1h`, `certificate: 24h`, `volume: 1h`, `db_instance: 15m`, `eip: 1h`, `key_pair: 24h`, `r2_bucket: 1h`, `default: 24h`.
- `output`: default output format.
- `profiles.<name>.platforms.<platform>`: the profile's account on one platform; `<platform>` is one of `cloudflare`, `namecom`, `godaddy`, `aws`, `gcp`, `aliyun`. A profile holds at most one account per platform.
- `profiles.<name>.platforms.<platform>.metadata`: non-secret values the provider needs (account id, region, project, login method...). Populated by `auth login`; you can edit them by hand.
- `profiles.<name>.platforms.<platform>.credential_backend`: where the secret was written (`keyring` or `file`). Written by polyctl, also when a refreshed token moves the secret from the file to the keyring. `auth rename` and `auth logout` use it to tell whether the secret is within reach in this run.
- `profiles.<name>.platforms.<platform>.created_at` / `updated_at`: when the account was first added and last logged in.
- `profiles.<name>.platforms.<platform>.legacy_secret`: written by polyctl only, while an account converted from a 0.1.x config still has its secret under the old key (see [Upgrading from 0.1.x](#upgrading-from-01x)). It disappears once the secret has been moved, or once the backend that would hold it says there is none.
- `profiles.<name>.platforms.<platform>.legacy_backend`: written by polyctl only, next to `legacy_secret`, when the account was logged in again while the backend holding the old entry was out of reach: it names that backend, so the old entry can still be removed once the backend is available.

- `profiles.<name>.defaults.<platform>.<key>`: overrides `defaults.<platform>.<key>` for the accounts of this profile (see below).

Profile names must not contain spaces or slashes.

#### Defaults, naming, hooks and certificates

These keys configure the commands that create resources. None of them holds a secret, and no built-in default is a value of a particular account (no account name, public key, address, VPC or security group id): a value that has no neutral default and is not configured has to be passed as a flag, otherwise the command is a usage error (exit code 2) that names the flag and the key.

```yaml
defaults:                         # per-platform defaults: defaults.<platform>.<key>
  aws:
    region: eu-west-1
    vpc_id: vpc-0123456789abcdef0
    subnet_id: subnet-0123456789abcdef0
    security_group_ids: [sg-0123456789abcdef0]
    key_name: ops
    instance_type: t3.small   # aws ec2 instance create --type (a type or a size such as 2H4G)
    elb_security_group_ids: [sg-0fedcba9876543210]   # aws elbv2 load-balancer ensure --sg
    tags: { team: ops }
    rds:                          # aws rds instance create
      default_class: db.t3.micro  # --class (built-in db.m5.large)
      default_major: "8.0"        # --major (built-in 8.0)
      default_storage_type: gp3   # --storage-type (built-in gp3)
      subnet_group: default       # --subnet-group
      security_group_ids: [sg-0123456789abcdef0]   # --security-group
      parameter_group: default.mysql8.0            # --parameter-group
      default_tags: { backup: daily }              # added to Name=<id> and defaults.aws.tags
    acm:
      default_key_algorithm: EC_prime256v1   # aws acm certificate request --key-algorithm
      default_key_types: [EC_prime256v1, RSA_2048]   # aws acm certificate list / get --key-type
      wildcard: true                         # aws acm certificate request --wildcard
  gcp:
    network: default
    subnet: default
    zone: europe-west1-b
    machine_type: e2-small   # gcp compute instance create --machine-type (a type or a size such as 2H4G)
    tags: [web]
    lb_network_tag: lb-health-check
    ssh_keys: []
  aliyun:
    region: cn-hongkong
    resource_group: rg-0123456789   # a name or an rg- id (aliyun eip address allocate / ensure, aliyun alb)
    eip:
      bandwidth: 200              # Mbps of the EIPs polyctl allocates
      isp: BGP_PRO
    ecs_instance_type: ecs.u1-c1m1.large   # aliyun ecs instance create / ensure --type (a type id or a size such as 2H4G)
    ecs_key_pair: ops               # aliyun ecs instance create / ensure: the key pair used without a key flag (when it exists in the region)
    ecs_security_group_rules: ["icmp:all:0.0.0.0/0", "tcp:80:0.0.0.0/0", "tcp:443:0.0.0.0/0"]   # aliyun ecs instance ensure --rule
    cas_region: ap-southeast-1      # aliyun cas: the CAS region (default: ap-southeast-1 for a cn-hongkong profile, the central store otherwise)
    cas_endpoint: cas.ap-southeast-1.aliyuncs.com   # aliyun cas: the CAS endpoint (default: the one of cas_region)
  cloudflare:
    ssl: strict

profiles:
  acme:
    platforms: { ... }
    defaults:                     # per-profile overrides: profiles.<name>.defaults.<platform>.<key>
      aws:
        instance_type: t3.large

naming:                           # names of the resources ensure commands create
  aws_elbv2_load_balancer: "{name}-lb"               # names are how aws elbv2 load-balancer ensure finds its resources again
  aws_elbv2_target_group: "{name}-{port}-target"
  aws_windows_key_pair: "{name}-win"               # RSA key pair aws ec2 instance create --os windows creates
  aliyun_alb_load_balancer: "lb-{name}"
  aliyun_alb_server_group: "{service}-{protocol}"
  aliyun_vpc: "{region}-vpc"
  aliyun_vswitch: "{region}-switch-{zone}"
  aliyun_ecs_security_group: "sg-common"
  gcp_lb_prefix: "lb-"                 # prefixes of the components of gcp compute load-balancer ensure
  gcp_instance_group_prefix: "ig-"
  gcp_backend_service_prefix: "bs-"
  gcp_proxy_prefix: "proxy-"
  gcp_forwarding_rule_prefix: "fr-"
  gcp_instance_external_ip: "{name}-external-ip"   # static addresses of gcp compute instance create / resize
  gcp_instance_internal_ip: "{name}-internal-ip"
  gcp_lb_firewall: "allow-lb-health-check-{network}"   # rule of gcp compute firewall ensure --preset lb-health-check
  gcp_ssl_certificate: "{slug}-cert-{date}"   # certificates of gcp compute load-balancer cert set and ssl-certificate update

hooks:
  on_failure: https://hooks.example.com/polyctl   # or a shell command

cert:
  email: you@your-domain.tld      # ACME account address (CAs refuse example.com addresses)
  agree_tos: false                # must be set to true explicitly
  dir: ""                         # default <config dir>/certs; a leading ~ is the home directory
  acme_directory: https://acme-v02.api.letsencrypt.org/directory
  key_type: ec256                 # ec256 | ec384 | rsa2048 | rsa4096
  reuse_key: false                # keep the private key on renewal
  renew_days: 30                  # default of --days
  name_template: "upload_{name}_{not_after}"
  skip_accounts: []               # accounts (or account:deployer) certificate deployment leaves alone
  dns:
    resolvers: ["1.1.1.1:53", "8.8.8.8:53", "223.5.5.5:53", "114.114.114.114:53"]
    min_resolvers: 2              # how many of them must see a TXT record
    propagation_timeout: 5m
    poll_interval: 10s
    settle_delay: 20s
  aws:
    regions: [us-east-1]          # regions certificate deployment scans (default: the account's region)
  aliyun:
    regions: [cn-hongkong]

dns:                              # live queries of `domain ns check`
  resolvers: [1.1.1.1, 1.0.0.1, 8.8.8.8, 8.8.4.4]   # IP or IP:port, asked in order
  timeout: 5s                     # per query

find:                             # polyctl find
  concurrency: 8                  # accounts --refresh syncs at the same time
  detail_timeout: 10s             # live lookup of one match with --detail
```

- `defaults.<platform>.<key>`: the value a command uses when its flag is not given. `<platform>` is a platform id (`aws`, `gcp`, `aliyun`, `cloudflare`, `namecom`, `godaddy`; aliases are not accepted here), and a key may be nested (`defaults.aws.rds.default_class`). Values are single values, lists or mappings, as shown. A profile overrides them with `profiles.<name>.defaults.<platform>.<key>`, which wins for that profile's accounts. The order is the usual one: the command-line flag, then the profile's value, then the top-level value, then the built-in default. Only two keys have a built-in default: `defaults.gcp.lb_network_tag` (`lb-health-check`) and `defaults.cloudflare.ssl` (`strict`; it takes `flexible`, `full` or `strict`, and any other value is reported when the config is loaded, exit code 2).
- `naming.<key>`: the template of the names `ensure` commands (and `aws ec2 instance create --os windows`, for its key pair) give to resources they create, unless a flag names the resource. Templates use `{name}`, `{service}`, `{protocol}`, `{port}`, `{region}`, `{zone}`, `{network}`, `{slug}` and `{date}`; which ones a resource provides is documented with its command, and a template that uses a variable the resource does not provide is an error. The defaults are the ones shown. An unknown key, an unknown variable or an unbalanced brace is reported when the config is loaded (exit code 2).
- `hooks.on_failure`: called when an `ensure`-style command fails (not for usage errors, not in a dry run), typically from cron. A value starting with `http://` or `https://` is a webhook: it receives a `POST` with a JSON body `{"event": "<command>.failed", "platform", "profile", "target", "domain", "error", "time", "changes"}` (`cert deploy` adds `"deployer"` and sends one event per failed target). Any other value is a shell command (`/bin/sh -c`, `cmd /C` on Windows) that gets the same JSON on stdin and the fields in `POLYCTL_EVENT`, `POLYCTL_PLATFORM`, `POLYCTL_PROFILE`, `POLYCTL_DEPLOYER`, `POLYCTL_TARGET`, `POLYCTL_DOMAIN` and `POLYCTL_ERROR`. A hook gets 15 seconds; a hook that fails or times out is a warning on stderr and never changes the exit code. Empty (the default): no notification.
- `cert.*`: the settings of the certificate commands, with the defaults shown (see [Certificates](#certificates)). `cert.name_template` names the certificates `cert deploy` uploads to Alibaba Cloud CAS (`{name}`, the root domain, and `{not_after}`, the expiry date); `cert.skip_accounts` lists the profiles (`name`) or deployers of a profile (`name:aliyun-alb`) it leaves alone, and `cert.aws.regions` / `cert.aliyun.regions` the regions it scans (default: the profile's region). `cert.email` and `cert.agree_tos` are only used to register the ACME account on the first issuance. `cert.dir` is the certificate store. `cert.acme_directory` is the CA (Let's Encrypt production by default). `cert.key_type` (`ec256`, `ec384`, `rsa2048` or `rsa4096`) is the default of `--key-type` and `cert.renew_days` the default of `--days`. `cert.reuse_key: true` keeps the private key on renewal instead of creating a new one. `cert.dns.*` tune the DNS-01 challenge: the resolvers (IP or IP:port) asked whether the TXT records are visible, how many of them must see them (`min_resolvers`, at most the number of resolvers), the longest wait (`propagation_timeout`), the interval between checks (`poll_interval`) and the pause before the CA validates (`settle_delay`, also waited after stale challenge records were deleted).
- `dns.resolvers` / `dns.timeout`: the recursive resolvers `domain ns check` asks, in order, and the timeout of one query, with the defaults shown; `--resolver` and `--timeout` override them. A resolver that is not an IP address or IP:port, or a negative timeout, is reported when the config is loaded (exit code 2).
- `find.concurrency` / `find.detail_timeout`: how many accounts `find --refresh` syncs at the same time, and how long `find --detail` waits for the subnet of one match, with the defaults shown (see [Finding resources by IP or DNS name](#finding-resources-by-ip-or-dns-name)). A negative value is reported when the config is loaded (exit code 2).

Files written by 0.1.x, where a profile was a single platform (`profiles.<name>.platform: cloudflare` with `metadata` next to it), are still read: each such profile becomes a profile holding that one platform. See [Upgrading from 0.1.x](#upgrading-from-01x). A profile that mixes the two layouts is rejected when the config is loaded (exit code 2) instead of being loaded with a part of it dropped: both a `platform:` and a `platforms:` key (keep only `platforms:`), a `platform:` key without a value, or `metadata`, `credential_backend`, `created_at` or `updated_at` set on the profile itself without `platform:` (move them under `platforms.<platform>`).

polyctl reads `config.yaml` when a command starts and writes it back as a whole. If the file was changed in between, by another polyctl process or by hand, the command does not save its older copy over that change: it fails with "the config file was changed since it was read", changes nothing in the file, and can simply be run again. `auth login` reads the file again after its login flow, which can wait for you for minutes, so changes made meanwhile are kept and the login is still saved. `auth logout` checks the file before it removes anything: the interactive form reads it again after its last question and removes what is still there (an entry that is gone by then is skipped with a note), and `auth logout --profile` works on the file as it is when the removal starts. A removal that still finds the file changed stops with nothing removed.

### Credential storage

`auth login` stores the secret of each account as one keyring entry (service `polyctl`, account = `<profile>/<platform>`, for example `acme/cloudflare`) through `github.com/zalando/go-keyring`:

- macOS: Keychain
- Windows: Credential Manager
- Linux / BSD: Secret Service (GNOME Keyring, KWallet via the D-Bus API)

When the keyring cannot be reached (headless servers, containers, no D-Bus session), polyctl falls back to `<config dir>/credentials.json` with mode `0600` and prints a one-line warning. Reads consult both backends, so an account created on a headless box keeps working after a keyring becomes available, and vice versa. `auth logout` removes the secret from every backend. In the file backend the entries of `credentials.json` are keyed the same way. Changes to stored secrets are serialised between polyctl processes with a lock on `credentials.lock`, so commands started in parallel (from a script, say) do not lose each other's entries.

Force a backend with `credential_store: file` / `keyring` in `config.yaml` or the `POLYCTL_CREDENTIAL_STORE` environment variable.

The same store holds one more entry, `/instance-secrets` (a name no profile can have): the data key that encrypts the instance login credentials kept in `inventory.db` (see below). `auth logout` does not touch it.

### `inventory.db` schema

The inventory is a WAL-mode SQLite database with five tables:

```sql
CREATE TABLE resources (
  platform      TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- cloudflare, aws, ...
  profile       TEXT    NOT NULL,
  resource_type TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- see the resource types under Caching
  resource_id   TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- platform-native id (domain name for type domain)
  name          TEXT    NOT NULL,
  parent_id     TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',   -- zone id for dns_record
  domain        TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',   -- apex / zone name used for routing
  data          TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- JSON, the platform-native object
  updated_at    INTEGER NOT NULL,     -- unix seconds
  expires_at    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (platform, profile, resource_type, resource_id)
);

CREATE TABLE domain_index (            -- domain -> owning resource (zones and registered domains only)
  domain        TEXT NOT NULL,
  platform      TEXT NOT NULL,
  profile       TEXT NOT NULL,
  resource_type TEXT NOT NULL,
  resource_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
  updated_at    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (domain, platform, profile, resource_type, resource_id)
);

CREATE TABLE ip_index (                -- address / prefix / DNS name -> resource (polyctl find)
  platform      TEXT    NOT NULL,
  profile       TEXT    NOT NULL,
  resource_type TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- instance, load_balancer, eip, db_instance
  resource_id   TEXT    NOT NULL,
  ip            TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- canonical address, masked prefix ("10.1.2.0/24") or DNS name
  kind          TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- internal, external, dns, reserved
  prefix_bits   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,   -- prefix length of a prefix, 0 otherwise
  PRIMARY KEY (platform, profile, resource_type, resource_id, ip, kind)
);

CREATE TABLE instance_secrets (        -- Windows logins of created instances, sealed (see below)
  platform      TEXT    NOT NULL,
  profile       TEXT    NOT NULL,
  location      TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- region, or zone where instances live in one
  instance_id   TEXT    NOT NULL,
  name          TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- instance name when stored
  user          TEXT    NOT NULL,     -- login user name (not secret)
  nonce         BLOB    NOT NULL,     -- AES-GCM nonce, fresh for every write
  secret        BLOB    NOT NULL,     -- AES-256-GCM ciphertext of the password (and an AWS private key)
  created_at    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  updated_at    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (platform, profile, location, instance_id)
);

CREATE TABLE sync_state (              -- freshness of each synced set
  platform      TEXT    NOT NULL,
  profile       TEXT    NOT NULL,
  resource_type TEXT    NOT NULL,
  scope         TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',   -- '' = whole type, or a zone id for per-zone record sets
  synced_at     INTEGER NOT NULL,
  expires_at    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (platform, profile, resource_type, scope)
);
```

`resources.data` keeps each object in its platform-native shape (serialised as JSON), so `-o json` on a cached read shows the same fields a live read would. The version of that JSON is kept in SQLite's `user_version`; whenever a database is opened that still holds DNS records written by 0.1.x, their `content` field is renamed to `value`, so they keep their values without a new sync. `domain_index` is rebuilt from `zone` and `domain` resources on every sync; DNS records are never indexed, they are looked up through their zone. `ip_index` is written with the resources that carry addresses (every network interface, secondary and alias address of an instance, a load balancer's addresses and DNS name, an elastic / static address, a database endpoint) and rebuilt with their set; prefixes (GCP alias ranges, IPv6 prefixes) match every address they contain. A database created by an older version gets the table on first use and fills it on the next sync of those types. Apart from `instance_secrets`, the file is a cache and safe to delete at any time: polyctl recreates it and re-fetches on demand. `instance_secrets` is not a cache: a sync, `auth logout` (which purges the profile's cached rows) and `--refresh` leave it alone, `auth rename` moves its rows to the new name, and deleting the file loses the stored Windows passwords, which no platform can show again.

`polyctl inventory path` prints the database actually in use (`POLYCTL_INVENTORY_DB` when set, otherwise `<config dir>/inventory.db`).

### Instance login credentials

Linux instances log in with an SSH key only. Every Linux create of `aws ec2 instance`, `gcp compute instance` and `aliyun ecs instance` (and `aliyun ecs instance ensure`) sends a key, never a password: polyctl has no password flags for Linux, sends no password, password inheritance or user data that would set one, and a Linux create without any usable key is exit code 2 before anything is created (for an AWS or Alibaba Cloud create before anything is read, except that an AWS create with `--ami` first reads the image to tell Windows from Linux; on those two a terminal asks for a public key file instead).

A Windows instance logs in with a user name and a password. polyctl keeps the ones it sets or retrieves when it creates a Windows instance in the `instance_secrets` table of `inventory.db`, so that the login is known right after the create:

- The user name is stored as it is; the password (and, for AWS, the private key that decrypts the password AWS generates) is sealed with AES-256-GCM: a random nonce for every write, the row's platform, location, instance id and user name as additional data (a ciphertext copied to another row does not open). Nothing secret is ever stored in plain text, in the inventory's other tables, in change reports, in `hooks.on_failure` payloads, in error messages or in `-v` output.
- The 32-byte data key is generated (from `crypto/rand`) the first time a credential is stored, and kept in the credential store under `/instance-secrets`: the OS keyring, or `credentials.json` (mode `0600`) where there is no keyring. It never enters `inventory.db`. Copying `inventory.db` to another machine without that entry, or removing the entry, makes the stored credentials unreadable: reading them is then exit code 1 with a message that says so, and polyctl does not replace the key on a read.
- An interactive create prints the user name and the password once it is done, clearly labelled: on stdout after the change report with the table output, on stderr (the terminal) with `-o json` / `-o yaml` so that stdout stays one document. The non-interactive flow never prints the password: a note on stderr names the user and the command that shows the password, `<tree> password <name|id>`, which prints `INSTANCE`, `USER` and `PASSWORD` (`-o json`: `{"instance", "id", "user", "password", "platform", "profile", "location", "updated_at"}`); an instance with nothing stored is exit code 4. When the credentials cannot be stored, an interactive create prints them with a warning that they cannot be shown again, and a non-interactive one fails with exit code 1 (without the password), saying how to set a new password: in the platform's console, or on Compute Engine with `gcp compute instance password <id> --reset`.
- `--dry-run` generates no password, stores nothing and creates no data key. Deleting an instance through polyctl removes its stored credentials once the delete succeeded.
- Alibaba Cloud ECS (`aliyun ecs instance create` / `ensure --os windows`, or an `--image` whose `OSType` is `windows`): the user is `Administrator` and polyctl generates the password, which `RunInstances` sets. `aliyun ecs instance password <name|id> [--region <r>]` reads the local database only; Alibaba Cloud never shows an instance password, so for an instance polyctl did not create (exit code 4) reset the password in the ECS console. `ensure` on an existing instance never changes its password.
- Compute Engine (`gcp compute instance create --os windows`, an `--image` of the `windows-cloud` project or one with the `WINDOWS` guest OS feature): Windows images have no password of their own and no create-time password, so polyctl uses the documented password reset of the guest agent, as `gcloud compute reset-windows-password` does. Once the instance is `RUNNING`, create generates an RSA-2048 key (kept in memory only), adds a line `{"userName", "modulus", "exponent", "email", "expireOn"}` with its public half (base64; `expireOn` RFC 3339, `--password-timeout` ahead, at least 5 minutes) to the instance's `windows-keys` metadata (the other metadata and the unexpired entries are kept, sent back with the metadata fingerprint), and reads serial port 4 every 10 seconds, from the position it had before the request, for the agent's answer to that modulus: the agent creates the user (`--windows-user`, `polyctl-admin` by default: 1 to 20 characters without spaces or `"/\[]:;|=,+*?<>@`, exit code 2 otherwise; an administrator) or updates its password, and writes the password encrypted with the key (RSA-OAEP, SHA-1), which polyctl decrypts and stores. The wait is bounded by `--password-timeout` (10 minutes by default). An agent that does not answer in time, an error it reports, a serial port that cannot be read (`compute.instances.getSerialPortOutput`), or `--no-wait`, leaves the create successful (exit code 0) with nothing stored and a note naming `gcp compute instance password <id> --reset`. `gcp compute instance password <name|id> [--zone <z>]` shows the stored credentials from the local database; with nothing stored it is exit code 4 with a hint to use `--reset`. `--reset` runs the same request again on a running Windows instance, also one polyctl did not create, for its stored user, else `polyctl-admin`, or `--user`: the old password stops working, so it asks for a confirmation (see Confirmations; a dry run reports `would update` and generates, writes and asks nothing); then it stores and shows the new password (the table or JSON of the password command, in both flows). A Linux instance is exit code 2 (never a password), a stopped one exit code 1, and an agent that does not answer within `--password-timeout` exit code 1. `--windows-user` and `--password-timeout` of create are exit code 2 with a Linux image.
- AWS EC2 (`aws ec2 instance create --os windows`, or an `--ami` whose platform is `windows`): the user is `Administrator`, and the password is the one the instance generates at its first boot, which AWS encrypts with the public key of the instance's RSA key pair (ED25519 key pairs cannot be used for Windows), so a Windows instance also launches with a key pair. Without `--key-file`, polyctl creates an RSA key pair for the instance (`CreateKeyPair`, PEM; named by `naming.aws_windows_key_pair`, `{name}-win`, with the tags of `defaults.aws.tags`; a key pair of that name already is exit code 2) and stores its private key sealed with the credentials, never printed. `--key-file <path>` (an unencrypted RSA private key, PEM or OpenSSH) uses the key pair that holds its public key instead, importing it under `--key-pair` when none does; that private key is never stored. Once the instance runs, create polls `GetPasswordData` every 15 seconds for up to `--password-timeout` (20 minutes by default), decrypts the password (base64, then RSA PKCS #1 v1.5) and stores it. A password not generated in time, `--no-wait`, or a password that cannot be read leaves the create successful (exit code 0) with a note, and `aws ec2 instance password <name|id> [--key-file <path>] [--region <r>]` fetches it later: stored credentials are shown as they are; otherwise it asks `GetPasswordData` again and decrypts with the stored private key or `--key-file`, then stores the password. A password AWS has not generated yet is exit code 4 ("try again in a few minutes"); an instance with nothing stored and no `--key-file`, or whose key pair is the user's and no `--key-file` is given, is exit code 4 with a hint; with `--key-file` it also fetches the password of a Windows instance polyctl did not create. `aws ec2 instance delete` also deletes the key pair polyctl created for the instance (the one recorded with its credentials, when the instance still uses it), never a key pair of the user's. A dry run creates no key pair and stores nothing.
- The passwords polyctl generates (Alibaba Cloud; AWS and Compute Engine instances generate their own) are 20 characters from `crypto/rand`, with upper- and lower-case letters, digits and at least one of `@#-_+=.,:` (accepted by Alibaba Cloud, AWS and Compute Engine Windows images, free of quotes, backslashes, spaces and shell or `cmd.exe` metacharacters), starting with a letter or digit, without the look-alikes `0 O 1 l I`.

## Interactive and non-interactive use

polyctl has two call flows, and every command behaves the same way within one of them.

- **Interactive**: a person at a terminal. A missing argument may be asked for with an arrow-key list, destructive actions ask for confirmation (default No), and login flows prompt for credentials and open a browser.
- **Non-interactive**: scripts, CI, pipes, cron. polyctl never shows a menu, never asks you to choose or confirm anything, never reads an answer from a terminal and never opens a browser, so it never waits for a person. What the interactive flow would have asked for has to be passed; when it is missing the command fails right away with a usage error (exit code 2) that names the argument, flag, `--set` key or environment variable to pass, and changes nothing.

The flow is decided once per command, in one place:

| Situation | Flow |
| --- | --- |
| stdin **and** stderr are terminals | interactive |
| stdin or stderr is not a terminal (pipe, redirect, `</dev/null`, CI, cron) | non-interactive |
| `--non-interactive`, or `POLYCTL_NON_INTERACTIVE` set to `1` / `true` / `yes` | non-interactive, also on a terminal |

`--yes` / `-y` means "assume yes for confirmations" in both flows. On a terminal it skips the confirmation questions only; a missing argument is still asked for. `--non-interactive --yes` together mean no question at all, and destructive actions proceed.

### Missing input in the interactive flow

In the interactive flow a missing argument or choice never ends the command, and polyctl never asks you to type a value it can list. The same rules hold for every command:

- **Pick from a list.** When a required argument or choice has no built-in or configured default, polyctl lists the candidates (from the platform, the local inventory or local files) in an arrow-key list on stderr: type to filter, Enter to choose. Each entry says what it is (name, id and the facts that tell them apart: region or zone, status, fingerprint, expiry, account), and the sensible one is preselected (the current profile, the key pair that holds your `~/.ssh` key, the opposite of a switch's current state).
- **Only usable candidates.** The lists hold what can be used for the request: available or active items only, never deprecated or retired ones, never arm64 types or images (many server packages still lack arm64 builds), and only what fits the choices already made (the region, zone, VPC, zone or bucket chosen before).
- **Create a new one.** Where polyctl can create the resource, the list ends with "Create a new ..." or "Import a new ...", which asks only what that needs: a new name is typed and checked as you type (taken, too short, invalid characters), with no default name for things that may be somebody else's (key pairs).
- **Defaults are not asked.** A configured default (`defaults` in `config.yaml`, the profile's region or project, naming templates) is used without a question, and so is a built-in default (disk size, instance type, image, TTL).
- **Ambiguity is a choice.** A name that matches several resources, a domain owned by several accounts, or a platform configured in several profiles (none current) is asked about instead of failing with exit code 5.
- **Values that cannot be listed** (a record value, an address to find, a new hostname) are typed, checked as they are typed.
- **Nothing to choose.** An empty list is a `nothing to do: ...` note and exit code 0 for a command that acts on an existing resource, and a usage error (exit code 2) naming the flag for an input a create cannot do without.
- **Leaving** a list or a question with Esc, Ctrl-C or Ctrl-D prints `cancelled`, changes nothing and exits 0. `--dry-run` still asks for missing input (it reads, it does not write); `--yes` answers confirmations only and never chooses anything.

The non-interactive flow is unchanged by all of this: it never asks, and a missing input is a usage error (exit code 2) naming what to pass, raised before anything is written.

In the non-interactive flow stdin is data, not a conversation: a piped `y` does not confirm anything and piped lines do not answer questions. stdin is read only where a command says so: `--set key=-` on `auth login` reads the value of that one key (one line per such key, in the order given, no label printed), and nothing else: no login reads a token from stdin on its own, so a pipe that stays open without data never blocks a command. Notes and errors are plain lines on stderr (no colours, no drawing), results go to stdout.

What each command asks, and what it needs instead:

| Command | Interactive flow asks | Non-interactive flow requires |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `auth login` | the platform, from a list | `auth login <platform>` |
| `auth login <platform> --set key=-` | the value of `key`, typed at a prompt (hidden for secrets) | the value piped on stdin, one line per `key=-` |
| `auth use` | the profile, from a list | `auth use <profile>` |
| `auth login <platform>` without `--profile`, when only other profiles have the platform | re-authenticate it in one of those profiles, add another account to the current profile, or a new profile (its name checked as typed) | `--profile <name>` |
| `auth rename` | the profile (current one preselected), then the new name (checked as typed) | `auth rename <old> <new>` |
| any platform command, when several profiles hold an account on the platform and none is current or given | the profile, from those holding an account (asked once per run) | `--profile <name>` |
| any command whose domain, zone or resource name matches several owners or resources | which one, from the matches with their accounts and ids | `--profile` / `--platform`, or the id (exit code 5 otherwise) |
| `dns list` / `set` / `add` / `ensure` / `delete` without `<domain>` | the zone, from the indexed zones (restricted by `--profile` / `--platform`) | `<domain>` |
| `dns set` / `add` without `--type` / `--value` | the record type (from the types the platform writes), then the value, checked as typed (`dns set` shows the current value) | `--type` and `--value` |
| `dns delete <domain>` without `--type`, `--id` or `--all` | the records to delete (multiple choice; the zone's SOA and apex NS are not offered, records owned by another service are marked; `--name` and `--value` narrow the list) | `--type` (with `--name`), `--id` or `--all` |
| `domain info` / `which` / `ns check` / `ns get` / `ns set` without `<domain>` | the domain, from the index (`ns get` / `ns set`: registered domains) | `<domain>` |
| `domain ns set <domain>` without nameservers (also `namecom` / `godaddy domain ns set`) | the nameserver set of a DNS zone of the domain known to the index, or "Enter nameservers..." | `<nameserver>...` |
| `find` without the query | the IP address or DNS name, checked as typed | `<ip\|dns-name>` |
| `cert ensure` without `<domain>` | the domain, from the indexed DNS zones, with the state of its certificate in the store | `<domain>` |
| `cert get` without `<domain>` | the certificate, from the store (key type, expiry, days left) | `<domain>` |
| `cert deploy` without `<domain>` or `--all` | "Every certificate that is due (--all)" or one certificate of the store | `<domain>` or `--all` |
| `auth logout [platform]` | the accounts to remove (multiple choice), "Remove N entries?" and "Keep cached inventory?"; `--yes` skips the two confirmations | `--profile <name>` (naming the profile is the confirmation; `--keep-inventory` keeps the cache) |
| `auth manage` | everything, in a menu | not available: use `auth list`, `auth use <profile>`, `auth login <platform>`, `auth status`, `auth rename <old> <new>`, `auth logout [platform] --profile <name>` |
| every command of [Confirmations](#confirmations) | its yes/no question (default no) after showing what it affects; `--yes` skips it | `--yes` |
| `cert ensure`, and `cert deploy` when it issues, the first time an ACME account is registered | the e-mail address of the account, then "Agree to the terms of service of the CA (...)?" | `--email` (or `cert.email`) and `--agree-tos` (or `cert.agree_tos: true`) |
| commands that bind a key pair (`aliyun ecs instance create` / `ensure`, `aliyun ecs key-pair ensure`, `aws ec2 key-pair ensure`, `aws ec2 instance create`) without `--ssh-key`, `--ssh-key-file` or `--key-pair`, and no configured default key pair (`defaults.aws.key_name`, `defaults.aliyun.ecs_key_pair`) | the key pair, from the existing ones of the region with their fingerprints (the one holding a `~/.ssh/*.pub` key marked and preselected), or "Import a new key pair from a public key file...": the file, from the usable `~/.ssh/*.pub` keys (type, fingerprint and comment shown, keys already registered marked, types the platform refuses left out) or a path, then the name of the new key pair (no default, checked as typed); the key pair to use when the key is registered under several names | `--key-pair` naming an existing key pair, or `--ssh-key` / `--ssh-key-file` with `--key-pair` to import one (exit code 2 without them; exit code 5 when the key is under several names and `--key-pair` picks none) |
| `aws rds instance create --password-stdin` | the master password, typed at a prompt without echo | the password piped on stdin (one line), or `--password-env <VAR>` |
| `auth login cloudflare` | the API token (after opening the token page); the account, when the token sees several | `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` or `--set token=-` (stdin is read only for `--set token=-`); `--set account_id=<id>` when the token sees several accounts |
| `auth login cloudflare --set method=oauth` | nothing typed: the approval in the browser (the URL is printed too; `--no-browser` only prints it), waiting up to 5 minutes; the account, when the authorization covers several | not available: a person has to approve, so it is a usage error naming `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` / `--set token=-` |
| `auth login namecom` | username and API token | `--set username=<user>` and `--set token=-` |
| `auth login godaddy` | the Personal Access Token (or, with `--set method=api_key`, key and secret) | `--set token=-`, or `--set api_key=<key> --set api_secret=-` |
| `auth login aws` | the method | `--set method=sso\|keys\|profile` |
| `auth login aws --set method=keys` | access key id, secret access key, optional session token | `--set access_key_id=<id> --set secret_access_key=-` (and `--set session_token=-`), or `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` + `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` (+ `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN`) |
| `auth login aws --set method=profile` | the AWS profile name (default `default`) | nothing: `--set aws_profile=<name>`, else the profile of the previous login, else `default` |
| `auth login aws --set method=sso` | start URL, Identity Center region; then, after the browser approval, the account and the role when several are accessible | `--set start_url=<url>` (the region defaults to `us-east-1`, or `--set sso_region=`); `--set account_id=<id>` and `--set role_name=<name>` when several are accessible |
| `auth login gcp` (`adc`) | the project, from the visible ones | nothing when the project is unambiguous (`--set project=<id>`, the previous login's, the ADC project, the only visible one); otherwise `--set project=<id>` |
| `auth login gcp --set method=service_account` | the key file path; the project when the key names none | `--set key_file=<path>`; `--set project=<id>` when the key names none |
| `auth login gcp --set method=oauth` | client id and secret; the project | `--set client_id=<id> --set client_secret=- --set project=<id>` |
| `auth login aliyun` | the site (`cn` or `intl`, from a list, no default), then AccessKey ID and Secret, then (once the key is verified) the default region, from the available regions of the account (the previous login's preselected) | `--set site=cn\|intl` (a re-authentication keeps the site of the first login) and `--set access_key_id=<id> --set access_key_secret=-`, or `ALIBABA_CLOUD_ACCESS_KEY_ID` + `ALIBABA_CLOUD_ACCESS_KEY_SECRET`, or `--set aliyun_profile=<profile>`; the region defaults to the previous login's, then `cn-hangzhou` |
| `cloudflare zone get` / `delete`, `dns list` / `create` / `update` / `delete`, `cache purge`, `cache rule` / `redirect rule list` / `set`, `dev-mode`, `ssl-mode`, `zone setting list` / `get` / `set` without `<zone>` | the zone (from the zones of the profile, or of every selected Cloudflare account for the commands that route a zone among them) | `<zone>` |
| `cloudflare dns create` / `namecom dns create` / `godaddy dns add` / `upsert` without `--type` / `--value` | the record type, then the value, checked as typed | `--type` and `--value` |
| `cloudflare dns update` / `delete`, `namecom dns update` / `delete` without `<record-id>`, `godaddy dns delete` without `--type` / `--id` | the record, from the records of the zone (name, type, value, TTL, id; records owned by an R2 bucket marked) | `<record-id>` (`godaddy`: `--type` or `--id`) |
| `cloudflare cache purge` without `--everything` or a selection | everything, or URLs / hosts / tags / prefixes (then typed, checked) | `--everything` or `--url` / `--host` / `--tag` / `--prefix` |
| `cloudflare zone setting get` / `set` without `<setting>` / `<value>` | the setting, from the zone's settings with their values (editable ones for `set`, plus `argo_smart_routing` and `foundation_dns`); the value from the allowed ones (on/off with the opposite of the current one preselected) or typed with the current one as default | `<setting>` and `<value>` |
| `cloudflare zone create` / `ensure` without `<domain>` | the domain, from the registered domains the index knows that have no Cloudflare zone yet, or "Another domain..." (checked as typed); the account when the profile is not pinned and its credentials see several | `<domain>`; `--set account_id` pinned at login when several accounts are visible |
| `cloudflare r2 domain list` / `get` / `attach` / `update` / `detach` / `ensure` without `<bucket>` / `<domain>` | the bucket (from the buckets of the selected accounts); for `get` / `update` / `detach` the custom domain, from those bound to the bucket; for `attach` / `ensure` the new hostname, checked as typed; `update` without `--enabled` / `--min-tls`: enable/disable or the minimum TLS version | `<bucket> <domain>`; `update`: `--enabled[=false]` and/or `--min-tls` |
| `namecom` / `godaddy domain get` / `ns get` / `ns set` / `lock` / `unlock` / `autorenew`, `dns list` / `create` / `add` / `upsert` / `update` / `delete` without `<domain>` | the domain or zone, from those of the profile (`lock`: unlocked ones, `unlock`: locked ones; none is nothing to do) | `<domain>` |
| `namecom` / `godaddy domain autorenew <domain>` without `on\|off` | on or off, the opposite of the current setting preselected | `on` or `off` |
| `namecom` / `godaddy domain check` without names | the names, typed and checked | `<name>...` |
| `aliyun dns record list` / `create` / `update` / `delete`, `aliyun domain get` / `ns get` / `ns set` without `<domain>` | the alidns zone (`dns record`) or the registered domain (`domain`), from those of the profile; `create` without `--type` / `--value`: the record type, then the value, checked as typed; `update` / `delete` without `<record-id>`: the record, from the records of the zone; `ns set` without nameservers: the nameserver set of an indexed DNS zone of the domain, or "Enter nameservers..." | `<domain>`; `create`: `--type` and `--value`; `update` / `delete`: `<record-id>`; `ns set`: `<nameserver>...` |
| `aliyun ecs instance create` without `<name>`, `--type` (and `defaults.aliyun.ecs_instance_type`), `--security-group`, or with no default vSwitch | the name (checked as typed); the vSwitch, from the Available vSwitches of the region, only when the default ones do not exist; the size, from the sizes of the x86 types in stock in the instance's zones (2H4G preselected; resolved to the cheapest type of the size); the security group, from the groups of the vSwitch's VPC (the `naming.aliyun_ecs_security_group` one preselected), or "Create a new security group..." (its name checked as typed; created with the rules of `ensure`) | `<name>`, `--type` (or the setting), `--security-group`, `--vswitch` when the default vSwitch does not exist |
| `aliyun ecs instance ensure` without `<name>` | the instance, from the instances of the region (read live), or "New instance name..." (checked as typed); a name several instances share: which one | `<name>` (exit code 5 for a name several instances share) |
| `aliyun ecs instance get` / `start` / `stop` / `reboot` / `delete` / `resize` without `<name\|id>` | the instance, from the inventory of the selected accounts (restricted to `--region`; `start`: Stopped ones, `stop`: Running or Starting, `reboot`: Running, `resize`: Running or Stopped; none is nothing to do) | `<name\|id>` |
| `aliyun ecs instance resize` without `--type` | the size, from the sizes of the x86 types in stock that the instance can change to in its zone (GPU types and its own size left out), resolved like `--type <cores>H<memory>G` to the cheapest type of that size | `--type` |
| `aliyun ecs instance password`, `aws ec2 instance password`, `gcp compute instance password` without `<name\|id>` | the instance, from those of the profile whose credentials are stored (none is nothing to do; `gcp ... --reset`: every instance of the inventory); a name stored for several instances: which one | `<name\|id>` (exit code 5 for a name stored for several) |
| `aliyun ecs security-group get` / `delete` / `rule add` / `rule remove` without the group | the group, from the groups of the region (of `--vpc` when given; none is nothing to do) | `<name\|id>` / `<sg>` |
| `aliyun ecs security-group rule add` without `--rule` | the common rules the group lacks (`tcp:ssh`, `tcp:http`, `tcp:https`, `icmp:all`, from `0.0.0.0/0`; multiple choice) and "Other rules (typed)...", one rule per line checked as typed, an empty line ending | `--rule` |
| `aliyun ecs security-group rule remove` without `--rule` / `--rule-id` | the rules to remove, from the rules of the group (multiple choice; none is nothing to do) | `--rule` or `--rule-id` |
| `aliyun ecs security-group create` without `--vpc` / `<name>` | the VPC, from the Available VPCs of the region (the `naming.aliyun_vpc` one preselected); the name, checked as typed against the groups of that VPC | `--vpc` and `<name>` |
| `aliyun ecs security-group ensure` without `<name>`, or when the default VPC does not exist | the group, from the groups of the region, or "New security group..." (its name checked as typed); the VPC, from the Available VPCs of the region | `<name>`; `--vpc` or `--instance` when the default VPC does not exist |
| `aliyun ecs key-pair import` without `--ssh-key` / `--ssh-key-file` / `<name>` | the public key file, from the usable `~/.ssh/*.pub` keys (keys already registered marked) or a path; the name (no default), checked as typed against the key pairs of the region | `<name>` and `--ssh-key` or `--ssh-key-file` |
| `aliyun eip address associate` without the EIP / `--instance` | the instance, from the Running or Stopped instances without a public address or EIP; the EIP, from the idle EIPs of its region (bound, busy and product-managed ones left out), or "Allocate a new EIP..." (with `defaults.aliyun.eip`; offered as a choice also when no EIP is idle) | `<allocation-id\|ip>` and `--instance` |
| `aliyun alb load-balancer get` / `update` / `delete` without `<name\|id>` | the load balancer, from the inventory of the selected accounts (restricted to `--region`; none is nothing to do); `update` without `--name` / `--deletion-protection`: what to change, then the new name (checked as typed) or on/off (the opposite of the current setting preselected) | `<name\|id>`; `update`: `--name` or `--deletion-protection` |
| `aliyun alb listener list` without `--lb`, `listener get` / `update` / `delete` / `cert set` without `<listener-id>` | the load balancer, from those of the region; then its listener (`cert set`: HTTPS and QUIC ones; none is nothing to do); `update` without `--server-group` / `--cert`: what to change, then the server group (the other Available ones of the load balancer's VPC) or the certificate | `--lb` / `<listener-id>` |
| `aliyun alb listener cert set` without `--cert` | the certificate, from the unexpired uploaded certificates of the region's CAS store (the newest other than the current one preselected, the current one marked) | `--cert` |
| `aliyun alb server-group get` / `update` / `delete` / `server add` without the group | the server group, from those of the region; `update` without any change flag: what to change (name checked as typed, scheduler, health check on with its path or off, session persistence; each with its current value); `server add` without `--instance`: the Running or Stopped instances of the group's VPC not in the group; without `--port`: the port, checked as typed (80, or 443 for an HTTPS group, proposed) | `<name\|id>` / `<server-group>`, `--instance`, `--port`; `update`: at least one change flag |
| `aliyun alb acl get` / `delete` / `entry add` / `entry remove` without the ACL | the ACL, from those of the region; `entry add` without entries: one address or CIDR block per line, checked as typed, an empty line ending; `entry remove` without entries: the entries of the ACL (multiple choice; none is nothing to do) | `<name\|id>` / `<acl> <cidr>...` |
| `aliyun cas certificate delete` without `<cert-id>` or `--expired` | "All expired certificates (N)" (same as `--expired`) or one uploaded certificate (name, names, expiry, id; none is nothing to do) | `<cert-id>` or `--expired` |
| `aliyun cas certificate upload` without `--cert` / `--key` / `<name>` | the certificate chain and the private key, as file paths checked as typed (the key against the certificate; `-` refused, standard input being the terminal); the name, `upload_<root domain>_<YYYY-MM-DD>` of the certificate proposed | `<name>`, `--cert` and `--key` |
| `aliyun alb listener create` without `--lb` / `--listener` / `--server-group`, or an HTTPS listener without `--cert` | the load balancer, from the Active ones of the region; the listener, `https:443` or `http:80` when the port is free, or "Other port..." (`<protocol>:<port>`, checked as typed); the server group, from the Available ones of the load balancer's VPC (none is exit code 2 naming `--server-group`); the certificate, as for `listener cert set` | `--lb`, `--listener`, `--server-group` (and `--cert` for HTTPS) |
| `aliyun alb server-group create` without `--vpc` / `--protocol` / `<name>`, `aliyun alb acl create` without `<name>` | the VPC, from the Available VPCs (the `naming.aliyun_vpc` one preselected); the protocol, HTTP (preselected) or HTTPS; the name, checked as typed against the groups of the VPC (or the ACLs of the region) | `--vpc`, `--protocol`, `<name>` |
| `aliyun alb load-balancer create` without `<name>` / `--vpc` / `--zone-mapping` | the name, checked as typed against the load balancers of the region; the VPC, from the Available VPCs (the `naming.aliyun_vpc` one preselected); per missing zone mapping an Available vSwitch of the VPC in a zone offering ALB and not used by the other mapping, then an idle EIP not used yet (the second of the first one's ISP); nothing to pick from is exit code 2 naming the flag (create never creates what it depends on; `ensure` does) | `<name>`, `--vpc` and two `--zone-mapping` |
| `aliyun alb load-balancer ensure` without `<domain>` / `--service` | the domain, checked as typed (no wildcard); the instance, from the Running instances of the profile's region (`--region`) | `<domain>` and `--service` |
| `aliyun vpc vpc get` / `vswitch get` without `<name\|id>` | the VPC or vSwitch, from those of the region (`vswitch get`: of `--vpc` when given; none is nothing to do) | `<name\|id>` |
| `aliyun vpc vpc create` without `<name>` | the name (the `naming.aliyun_vpc` one proposed when it is free), checked as typed against the VPCs of the region | `<name>` |
| `aliyun vpc vswitch create` without `--vpc` / `--zone` / `<name>` | the VPC, from the Available VPCs (the `naming.aliyun_vpc` one preselected); the zone, from the zones that offer instances (zones holding a vSwitch of the VPC marked, the first default zone without one preselected); the name (the `naming.aliyun_vswitch` one of the zone proposed), checked as typed | `--vpc`, `--zone` and `<name>` |
| `aliyun vpc vswitch ensure` when the default VPC does not exist | the VPC, from the Available VPCs of the region | `--vpc` |
| `aws ec2 instance get` / `start` / `stop` / `reboot` / `delete` / `resize` without `<name\|id>` | the instance, from the inventory of the selected accounts (restricted to `--region`; `get` / `delete`: every one not terminated, `start`: stopped ones, `stop`: running or pending, `reboot` / `resize`: running or stopped; none is nothing to do); a name several live instances share: which one | `<name\|id>` (exit code 5 for a name several live instances share) |
| `aws ec2 instance create` without `<name>`, `--subnet` (and `defaults.aws.subnet_id`) or `--type` (and `defaults.aws.instance_type`) | the name, checked as typed (no live instance of that name in the region); the subnet, from the available subnets of the region (the first default subnet of a zone preselected; none is exit code 2 naming `--subnet`); the size, from the T3 sizes (x86_64) offered in the subnet's zone (2H4G preselected), or "Other size or exact instance type..." (checked as typed) | `<name>`, `--subnet` and `--type` (or the settings) |
| `aws ec2 instance resize` without `--type` | the size, from the T3 sizes (x86_64) offered in the instance's availability zone (its own type left out, 2H4G preselected), or "Other size or exact instance type..." (checked as typed); an arm64 instance: an exact type, typed | `--type` |
| `aws ec2 key-pair import` without `--ssh-key` / `--ssh-key-file` / `<name>` | the public key file, from the usable `~/.ssh/*.pub` keys (keys already registered marked) or a path; the name (no default), checked as typed against the key pairs of the region | `<name>` and `--ssh-key` or `--ssh-key-file` |
| `aws ec2 security-group get` / `delete` / `rule add` / `rule remove` without the group | the group, from the groups of the region (of `--vpc` or `defaults.aws.vpc_id` when set; `delete`: not the VPC's default group; none is nothing to do); a name used in several VPCs: which one | `<sg-id\|name>` (exit code 5 for a name used in several VPCs) |
| `aws ec2 security-group rule add` without `--rule` | the common rules the group lacks (`tcp:ssh`, `tcp:http`, `tcp:https`, `icmp:all`, from `0.0.0.0/0`; multiple choice) and "Other rules (typed)...", one rule per line checked as typed, an empty line ending | `--rule` |
| `aws ec2 security-group rule remove` without `--rule` | the rules to remove, from the ingress rules of the group (multiple choice; none is nothing to do) | `--rule` |
| `aws ec2 security-group create` without `--vpc` (and `defaults.aws.vpc_id`) / `<name>` | the VPC, from the available VPCs of the region (the default VPC preselected; none is exit code 2 naming `--vpc`); the name, checked as typed against the groups of that VPC | `--vpc` (or the setting) and `<name>` |
| `aws ec2 security-group ensure` without `<name>`, or without a VPC (`--vpc`, `--instance`, `defaults.aws.vpc_id`) | the VPC, from the available VPCs of the region; the group, from the groups of the VPC, or "New security group..." (its name checked as typed) | `<name>`; `--vpc`, `--instance` or the setting |

A re-authentication (`auth login` for an account the profile already has) reuses what the previous login recorded (username, start URL, account, role, project, region, method), so in the non-interactive flow usually only the secret has to be passed again.

Two login flows need a person in a browser but nothing typed into polyctl, so they stay available in the non-interactive flow: polyctl prints the URL, does not open a browser, and waits for a bounded time.

- `auth login aws --set method=sso` prints the verification URL and code and polls until the device code expires (the lifetime AWS gives the code; 10 minutes when it names none). If the approved login can access several accounts or roles and `--set account_id` / `--set role_name` were not given, it then fails with a usage error that lists them, so pass both in scripts.
- `auth login gcp --set method=oauth` prints the consent URL and waits up to 5 minutes for the browser to return to its loopback listener. On a terminal it waits until you press Ctrl-C.

`auth login cloudflare --set method=oauth` is not one of them: it runs in the interactive flow only, and is a usage error otherwise.

Commands that take no required input ask nothing in either flow: for example `auth list`, `auth status`, `sync`, `inventory`, `domain index`, `domain list` and every platform `list` without arguments. The platform trees follow the same rules for the rest of their commands; their own sections say what each one asks.

```sh
# scripts and CI: nothing is asked
printf '%s\n' "$NAMECOM_TOKEN" | polyctl auth login namecom --profile acme --set username=acme --set token=-
polyctl dns delete example.com --type A --name www --yes
polyctl aliyun ecs instance stop i-0123456789 --yes

# the same guarantees on your own terminal
polyctl --non-interactive auth use            # usage error: auth use: <profile> is required
POLYCTL_NON_INTERACTIVE=1 polyctl dns delete example.com --type A --name www
#   usage error: confirmation required: pass --yes to proceed (would delete 2 records in zone example.com)
```

### Confirmations

These commands ask one yes/no question (default no) before they delete, stop or overwrite something, after showing what is affected. `--yes` answers yes and nothing is asked, in both flows. In the non-interactive flow `--yes` is required: without it the command is a usage error (exit code 2) naming `--yes`. Declining, or leaving the question with Ctrl-C or Ctrl-D, changes nothing and is exit code 0 (`nothing to do`). A `--dry-run` asks nothing and needs no `--yes`. This is the one list of confirming commands; the other sections point here.

- Core: `dns delete` when the selector matches several records (they are listed first, also with the refusal), `dns delete --all` (refused before the zone is read), and `cert deploy --prune-expired` before it deletes expired certificates.
- Accounts: the interactive `auth logout` (its two confirmations) and the removal inside `auth manage`. In the non-interactive flow `auth logout` needs `--profile` instead, and `auth manage` is not available.
- AWS: `ec2 instance stop` (not asked for an instance stopped already; refused before any lookup), `ec2 instance delete` (alias `terminate`), `ec2 instance resize` of a running instance (a stopped one needs no confirmation), `ec2 security-group delete` and `rule remove`, `ec2 volume detach` (`--force` always needs `--yes`) and `delete`, `elbv2 load-balancer delete`, `target-group delete` and `listener delete`, and `rds instance delete`.
- GCP: `compute instance stop` (not asked for an instance stopped already; refused before any lookup), `delete` (alias `terminate`), `reboot` (a hard reset), `resize` of a running instance and `password --reset` (refused before any lookup), `compute firewall delete`, `compute ssl-certificate delete`, `compute load-balancer delete`, and the `delete` of `compute backend-service`, `forwarding-rule`, `health-check` and `instance-group`.
- Alibaba Cloud: `ecs instance stop` (refused before the account is opened), `delete` (alias `terminate`), `reboot --force` (a forced stop; a plain reboot asks nothing) and `resize` of a running instance, `ecs security-group delete` and `rule remove` (nothing to remove asks nothing), `alb load-balancer delete`, `listener delete`, `server-group delete` and `acl delete`, and `cas certificate delete`.
- Cloudflare: `r2 domain detach`, `zone delete`, and `cache rule set --replace` / `redirect rule set --replace` when they would delete rules.

## Accounts

A **profile** is a named set of accounts, at most one per platform. It usually stands for one project or one customer: the profile `acme` holds acme's Cloudflare account, its name.com account and its AWS account, so one name selects all of them. Two accounts on the same platform (production and a test environment, two AWS organisations) go into two profiles. Commands:

```text
polyctl auth login <platform> [--profile <name>] [--set key=value ...]
polyctl auth list
polyctl auth use <profile>
polyctl auth status [--profile <name>]
polyctl auth rename <old> <new>
polyctl auth logout [platform] --profile <name> [--keep-inventory]
polyctl auth manage
```

- `auth login` runs the platform's login flow, stores the secret and writes the account's metadata to `config.yaml`. The account is added to `--profile` when given, otherwise to the current profile, otherwise (no profile is current, or `current_profile` names a profile that does not exist) to a profile named `default`; a profile that does not exist is created, and the target profile becomes current if none was. Logging in to a platform the profile already has re-authenticates that account and leaves the profile's other accounts alone. Without `--profile`, when the target profile has no account on the platform but another profile does, the interactive flow asks whether to re-authenticate it in one of those profiles, add another account to the target profile or log in to a new profile; the non-interactive flow changes nothing and fails with a usage error (exit code 2) that lists those profiles: pass `--profile <name>` to re-authenticate one of them, or `--profile <target>` to really add a second account on that platform. Platform-specific inputs are passed with `--set key=value` (repeatable); `--set key=-` reads the value of that key from stdin instead of the command line. Anything not given is asked in the interactive flow; in the non-interactive flow nothing is asked, no browser is opened, and a value or choice that is still missing is a usage error (exit code 2) naming the `--set` key (see [Interactive and non-interactive use](#interactive-and-non-interactive-use)). Every login flow verifies against the platform, so with `--offline` `auth login` fails with exit code 6 before anything is asked, read from stdin or stored. Platform names accept aliases (`cf`, `name.com`, `gd`, `google`, `alibaba`, ...). `--set` takes the keys of that platform only (see [Login flows per platform](#login-flows-per-platform)); an unknown key is a usage error (exit code 2) listing the valid ones.
- `auth list` shows one row per account: profile, platform, whether the profile is current (`*`), the credential backend and a summary of the metadata. A profile without accounts gets one row too, marked `(no accounts)`; in JSON and YAML that row has `name` and `current` only, no `platform`.
- `auth use` sets `current_profile`; an unknown profile is an error (exit code 4). In the interactive flow, `auth use` without a name asks which profile; when no profile is configured there is nothing to pick, which is a `nothing to do` note and exit code 0. In the non-interactive flow the name is required (usage error, exit code 2).
- `auth status` calls the platform of every account (or only those of `--profile`) to verify the stored credentials and prints the resolved identity (account, user, email, ARN, project...), one row per account. It exits with code 3 if any account fails. With `--offline` it only checks that credentials are present.
- `auth rename <old> <new>` renames a profile. Its accounts, their secrets (copied to the `<new>/<platform>` keys before the old entries are deleted) and their cached resources move to the new name, and `current_profile` follows when it was the renamed profile. An unknown `<old>` is an error (exit code 4); a `<new>` that already exists, is empty or contains spaces or slashes is a usage error (exit code 2). If a step fails before `config.yaml` is saved, the copies are removed and the profile keeps its old name. A profile with an account still marked `legacy_secret` (see [Upgrading from 0.1.x](#upgrading-from-01x)) cannot be renamed until the old entry has been moved or removed: the message names the backend that has to be available for that, or says to log in to the account again when the backend is not on record or the entry could not be moved. An old account that simply has no stored secret is not held back by this. The rename is also refused, with nothing changed, when an account's `credential_backend` names a backend that is not available in this run (for example `keyring` while only the file store is in use), because its secret could not be moved.
