```text
aliyun alb server-group create <name> --vpc <id|name> --protocol HTTP|HTTPS [--resource-group default] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb server-group update <name|id> [--name <new-name>] [--scheduler Wrr|Wlc|Sch] [--health-check[=false]] [--health-check-path <path>] [--sticky-session[=false]] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb server-group delete <name|id> [--yes] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb server-group server add <server-group> --instance <ecs-name|id> --port <n> [--region <r>]
aliyun alb acl list [--name <name>] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb acl get <name|id> [--region <r>]
aliyun alb acl create <name> [--resource-group default] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb acl delete <name|id> [--yes] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb acl entry add <acl> <cidr>... [--description <text>] [--region <r>]
aliyun alb acl entry remove <acl> <cidr>... [--region <r>]
aliyun cas certificate list [--name <text>] [--expired] [--expires-within <days>]
aliyun cas certificate upload <name> --cert <fullchain.pem> --key <privkey.pem>
aliyun cas certificate delete (<cert-id> | --expired) [--force] [--region <r>]... [--yes]
```

`aliyun ecs instance start` and `stop` find the instance like `get`. One already `Running` (`start`) or `Stopped` (`stop`) is `unchanged` and nothing is sent. `stop` asks for confirmation on a terminal (default No; declining is a `nothing to do` note and exit code 0) unless `--yes` is given. In the non-interactive flow it never asks: without `--yes` it is a usage error (exit code 2) raised before the account is opened. `start --wait` waits until the instance is `Running`, `stop --wait` until it is `Stopped` (polled every 2 seconds, bounded by `--timeout`, 10m by default). The output is a change report, as for `reboot`.

ECS instances are named by their id (`i-...`) or their name. Alibaba Cloud does not keep names unique and its name filter ignores case, so polyctl compares names case-sensitively and treats a name held by several instances as ambiguous (exit code 5: pass the id). Without `--region`, `get`, `delete`, `reboot` and `resize` find the instance through the local inventory of every selected account (synced once when it does not know the instance; the sync covers every region of the account, and `--region` asks one region directly) and then read it live; with `--region` the selected profile is asked in that region. A missing instance is exit code 4.

- `get <name|id>` shows `NAME`, `ID`, `REGION`, `TYPE`, `STATUS`, `PUBLIC_IP`, `PRIVATE_IP` and `EIP` (`yes`/`no`). JSON: `{"name", "id", "profile", "region", "zone", "type", "status", "public_ip", "private_ip", "eip": {"allocation_id", "ip"} | null, "vpc_id", "vswitch_id", "security_group_ids", "key_pair", "created_at", "cached"}`. The public address is the instance's own one, else its EIP's; the private address is the classic network address, else the primary address of the primary network interface. `created_at` is RFC 3339 (Alibaba Cloud keeps minutes only). With `--offline` the inventory is shown as it is (exit code 6 when it does not hold the instance).
- `create <name>` creates one instance from existing network resources: pay as you go, pay by traffic, no public address of its own (outbound bandwidth 0; the public address is an EIP). `--type` is required (or `defaults.aliyun.ecs_instance_type`): a type id, or a size such as `2H4G` (see instance sizes below). `--vswitch` (id or name) places it and decides its zone; by default it is the vSwitch `naming.aliyun_vswitch` names for zone `b` in the VPC `naming.aliyun_vpc` names, which has to exist (otherwise exit code 2). `--security-group` (name or id, required) is looked up in that vSwitch's VPC; a group of another VPC is exit code 1. On a terminal a missing name, `--type`, `--security-group` or default vSwitch is asked (see [Interactive and non-interactive use](#interactive-and-non-interactive-use)); a security group chosen to be created there is created with the rules of `ensure`. Without any key flag, `defaults.aliyun.ecs_key_pair` names the key pair to use when it exists in the region. The key pair is resolved like `key-pair ensure`, existing first: `--key-pair`, or the key pair holding `--ssh-key` / `--ssh-key-file`; a missing one is imported under `--key-pair` (a terminal asks for the name; the non-interactive flow never chooses one: exit code 2). A key pair is required: Linux instances log in with a key only, and the request never carries a password or `PasswordInherit`; without `--key-pair`, `--ssh-key`, `--ssh-key-file` or `defaults.aliyun.ecs_key_pair` a terminal lists the key pairs of the region (the one holding a `~/.ssh/*.pub` key marked and preselected) and "Import a new key pair from a public key file...", and the non-interactive flow is exit code 2 before anything is read (with `--image`, right after the image is read, since only the image tells Linux from Windows; still before anything is written). The image is `--image` (any image id, the way to use Ubuntu, Debian or any other system; it is read first with `DescribeImages` by id, whoever owns it, available images only: a missing or deprecated image is exit code 4, with a hint naming `polyctl aliyun ecs image latest` and `--os`), else the newest public image of `--os` (`alibaba-cloud-linux` by default, or `windows`; see `image latest`) for the architecture of the instance type: a size resolves to an x86 type, and a type id is looked up with `DescribeInstanceTypes` (a type the region does not list gets the x86_64 image, with a note); the image is picked before the size is priced, and the same id is priced and created. The system disk is `--disk` GiB (20 by default; at least the image's size) of `--disk-category` (`cloud_essd_entry` by default). A Windows image, from `--os windows` (Windows Server Datacenter, Chinese edition, x86_64 only: an arm64 type is exit code 2) or an `--image` whose `OSType` is `windows` (such as an English Windows Server image, which `--os windows` never picks), changes three things: no key pair is used (ECS key pairs are for Linux only: `--key-pair`, `--ssh-key` and `--ssh-key-file` are exit code 2, and no key pair is looked up or imported); instead polyctl generates the password of `Administrator` (no flag takes a password), sends it in the `RunInstances` form body (never in the URL), and stores the user name and password encrypted as soon as the instance id is known, so the instance is ready for RDP once it is `Running`: an interactive create prints them at the end, a non-interactive one prints a note naming `polyctl aliyun ecs instance password <id>` (see [Instance login credentials](#instance-login-credentials)); a dry run generates and stores nothing; `--disk` defaults to 40, the size of the Windows images (with a Windows `--image`, its size when larger), and a smaller `--disk` is exit code 2 (with a Windows `--image`, one smaller than the image); the host name also has its dots replaced by hyphens and is cut to 15 characters (a name that leaves only digits is exit code 2). The host name is the name with every character other than letters, digits, hyphens and dots replaced by a hyphen (`site_a` becomes `site-a`), cut to 64 characters and trimmed of leading and trailing hyphens and dots; a name that leaves fewer than 2 characters is exit code 2. Instance names hold 2 to 128 characters, no `*`, and may not start with `i-`. An instance of the name in the region is exit code 1 (use `ensure`). The request carries a `ClientToken` derived from its content, so a retried request never creates a second instance; when the token answers an instance of an earlier identical request that was deleted since, the request is sent once more under a new token. create waits until it is `Running` (by default; `--no-wait` returns at once, and `--eip` waits anyway), then `--eip` binds an EIP like `ensure` does. A zone without stock of the type is exit code 1 with a hint to pass `--vswitch` in another zone or another `--type`.
- `delete <name|id>` (alias `terminate`) shows the instance and its addresses and asks for a confirmation (default no; `--yes` skips it; the non-interactive flow without `--yes` is exit code 2 before the account is opened). A `Running` instance is stopped and deleted once `Stopped`; `--force` deletes it at once. A `Pending`, `Starting` or `Stopping` instance is waited for until it is `Running` or `Stopped` (`--timeout` bounds each wait). A new instance cannot be deleted for about 45 seconds after its creation (`IncorrectInstanceStatus.Initializing`): the delete is retried every 3 to 5 seconds for up to 2 minutes. Deletion protection is exit code 1 (polyctl never switches it off). `--wait` then waits until the instance is no longer listed (report detail `deleted; gone`). An EIP bound to the instance is not released: it becomes idle, stays billed (a note says so) and is reused by the next `ensure`. The Windows credentials polyctl stored for the instance are removed once it is deleted (a declined or failed delete keeps them).
- `reboot <name|id>` reboots a `Running` instance; any other status is exit code 1. `--force` is `ForceStop` (like pulling the power: unsaved data is lost), so it asks for a confirmation first (default no; `--yes` skips it, the non-interactive flow needs `--yes`: exit code 2, before any lookup; a declined confirmation is exit code 0), as `gcp compute instance reboot` does; a plain reboot asks nothing. `--wait` waits until it is `Running` again, counting a `Running` read only after the instance was seen leaving `Running` (or 30 seconds after the reboot); a `Stopped` read on the way is accepted for 30 seconds.
- `resize <name|id> --type <type|size>` changes the type. ECS changes the type of a `Stopped` instance only, so a `Running` one is stopped (after a confirmation, default no; `--yes` skips it, the non-interactive flow needs it: exit code 2), changed and started again. The instance ends in the power state it had: a `Stopped` one stays `Stopped` (and needs no confirmation) unless `--start`, and `--start=false` leaves a `Running` one `Stopped`. The stop is always waited for; `--wait` also waits until it is `Running` again. `--wait-port <n>` (implies `--wait`) then waits until that TCP port (22 for SSH) accepts connections on the public IP, the EIP or the private IP. `--start` and `--wait-port` work the same in `aws ec2 instance resize` and `gcp compute instance resize`. The same type is `unchanged`. A failed change leaves the instance `Stopped` with its old type (exit code 1, with the Alibaba Cloud message). A size (`--type 2H4G`) is resolved before anything changes: an instance of that size already is `unchanged`; otherwise the candidates are the x86 types of exactly that size in stock that the instance can change to in its own zone (`DescribeResourcesModification`), priced with its image and system disk, and the cheapest wins.
- `ensure <name>` makes sure the instance exists, is `Running` and holds an EIP; a second run changes nothing. An instance of the name (several are exit code 5) is used as it is: what it depends on is neither checked nor repaired, and a `--type` or `--disk` given that differs is reported `skipped` (`type differs: current X, want Y; use resize`), never changed (a size is compared with the vCPUs and memory of the instance: `size differs: current X (2H2G), want 4H8G; use resize`, and nothing is resolved); a `Stopped` instance is `skipped` (start it), a `Pending` or `Starting` one is waited for. Otherwise the whole chain is created as needed, in this order: the key pair (as for `create`), the VPC (`naming.aliyun_vpc`, `172.16.0.0/12`, waited for until `Available`), its vSwitches in zones `b` and `c` (`naming.aliyun_vswitch`, the first free `/22` blocks), the security group (`--security-group`, else `naming.aliyun_ecs_security_group`, `sg-common`) in that VPC with the `--rule` rules (else `defaults.aliyun.ecs_security_group_rules`, else `icmp:all:0.0.0.0/0`, `tcp:80:0.0.0.0/0` and `tcp:443:0.0.0.0/0`; missing rules are added, none removed), then the instance in the zone `b` vSwitch, or with a size in the vSwitch of the zone the size resolved to (with `--vswitch`, in that existing vSwitch instead, and the VPC and vSwitches are not ensured). The instance is `--type` (`defaults.aliyun.ecs_instance_type`, else `ecs.u1-c1m1.large`; a type id or a size) with the image, disk and login of `create` (a Linux instance its key pair; a new Windows instance, of `--os windows` or of an `--image` whose `OSType` is `windows` (read before the plan is made), a generated `Administrator` password, stored and shown as `create` does; an existing instance keeps its password). It is waited for until `Running` (polled every 2 seconds, `--timeout` 10m; a `Stopped` read right after the creation is normal and accepted for 30 seconds, a `Stopped` after `Running` is a failure); `--no-wait` returns at once and skips the EIP. Then (`--eip`, the default; `--eip=false` skips it) an instance without an EIP gets an idle EIP of the region, else a new one (see the EIPs below), bound and waited for until `InUse`; a binding refused because the EIP or the instance is not ready is retried every 3 seconds, 10 times. Nothing created is rolled back when a later step fails: run `ensure` again to go on (EIPs allocated for a load balancer whose creation failed are named on stderr: they stay idle and billed until the next run reuses them). Rows: `key-pair/<name>`, `vpc/<name>`, `vswitch/<name>`, `security-group/<name>`, `security-group/<name>/rule`, `instance/<name>`, `instance/<name> status`, `eip/<name>`. An instance of the name that the inventory knows in another account or region is a note on stderr. `-o json` adds `"result": {"instance_id", "public_ip"}` (`(pending)` for an instance a dry run would create). `--dry-run` reads everything (the VPC, vSwitches, group and rules, the image, the idle EIPs) and reports what a real run would do, with `(pending)` for ids that do not exist yet. The default group name `sg-common` starts like a security group id: the `aliyun ecs security-group` commands read `sg-...` as an id, so address that group there by its id.
- **Instance sizes.** `--type` of `create`, `ensure` and `resize` (and `defaults.aliyun.ecs_instance_type`) also takes a size `<cores>H<memory>G`: `2H2G`, `2H4G`, `4H8G`, `1H0.5G` (any case). Anything else is a type id, used as it is. A size is resolved on every run, in a `--dry-run` too, and never remembered, because stock and prices change: the candidates are the types `DescribeAvailableResource` lists for exactly that many vCPUs and GiB of memory for pay-as-you-go, I/O optimized instances, where both the zone and the type are `WithStock`; they are limited to the zones the instance may go to (the zone of `--vswitch`; else for `create` the zones whose default vSwitch exists, `b` and `c`, and for `ensure` zones `b` and `c`), and to x86 types (`DescribeInstanceTypes` with `CpuArchitecture=X86`, 10 types a call). Each candidate is priced by the hour (`DescribePrice`, with the image, the system disk, pay by traffic and no bandwidth of its own, as the instance is created; 4 queries at a time) and the cheapest wins; ties go to the type name, then the zone. A candidate the price query refuses as not available is left out; any other error stops the command. The system disk is `--disk-category` when `DescribeAvailableResource` (`SystemDisk`) offers it for that type and zone; otherwise, unless `--disk-category` was given (then the type is left out), the first offered of `cloud_essd_entry`, `cloud_essd`, `cloud_auto`, `cloud_efficiency`, `cloud_ssd`, with a note. The zone of the choice places the instance (its default vSwitch). The choice is printed on stderr (`2H4G -> ecs.e-c1m2.large (cn-hongkong-b, 0.0123 CNY/hour)`) and the change report shows the type with the size and price (`ecs.e-c1m2.large (2H4G, 0.0123 CNY/hour)`). The memory must match exactly: some types have 3.5 or 7.5 GiB, so `2H4G` does not find a 3.5 GiB type (write `2H3.5G`). Nothing in stock is exit code 4, with the zones that do have the size in stock when there are any; a size of 0 vCPUs or 0 GiB is exit code 2.

ECS security groups belong to one region (`--region`, the profile's region by default) and one VPC. polyctl creates VPC security groups only (type `normal`), and never sends a create without a VPC: Alibaba Cloud would then create a default VPC on its own.

- A group is named by its id (`sg-...`) or its name. Alibaba Cloud does not keep names unique, not even within one VPC: a name held by several groups is ambiguous (exit code 5: pass the id, or `--vpc`), a missing group is exit code 4. `--vpc` takes a VPC id or name (a name several VPCs share is ambiguous; a missing VPC is exit code 4).
- Rules use the format of every platform's firewall commands (see the AWS security groups below): `<protocol>:<port|from-to|alias|all>:<source>`, e.g. `icmp:all:0.0.0.0/0`, `tcp:http:0.0.0.0/0`, `tcp:8000-8999:10.0.0.0/8`, `tcp:1-65535:203.0.113.7`. They are written as ECS port ranges (`80/80`, `8000/8999`, `-1/-1` for `icmp` and `all`) with the policy `accept`, the NIC type `intranet` and `--priority` (1-100, default 1); ICMP from an IPv6 source is `ICMPv6`. A single IPv4 address is always sent as `/32`, because ECS keeps `203.0.113.7` and `203.0.113.7/32` as two rules. Malformed rules and priorities are exit code 2 before anything is sent (ECS answers some of them with misleading error codes).
- Rules are compared by protocol, ports, source (normalized: a bare IPv4 address equals its `/32`, host bits are masked, IPv6 is compressed) and policy; priority and description never count. Only ingress rules are read (`Direction=ingress`, every page) and managed.
- `list` shows `NAME`, `ID`, `VPC`, `RULES` (rules of both directions, as ECS counts them) and `DESCRIPTION`, from the local inventory per region; `--vpc <vpc-id>` keeps one VPC's groups (a VPC name is exit code 2). No groups is `[]`, a note on stderr and exit code 0.
- `get` shows the group and its ingress rules. In JSON: `{"name", "id", "vpc_id", "region", "type", "description", "profile", "rules": [{"id", "direction", "protocol", "port", "source", "policy", "priority", "description"}]}`; `protocol` and `policy` are lower case, `port` is the ECS port range and `source` is shown as ECS stores it.
- `create <name>` creates the group in `--vpc` (required; nothing is sent without it, exit code 2). A group of that name in the VPC is exit code 1 (use `ensure`). Names may not start with `sg-` and hold at most 128 characters.
- `ensure <name>` makes sure the group exists, holds the `--rule` rules and, with `--instance`, holds the instance; a second run changes nothing. The VPC is the instance's with `--instance` (another `--vpc` is exit code 2), else `--vpc`, else the VPC named by `naming.aliyun_vpc` (`{region}-vpc`), which has to exist (otherwise exit code 2 naming it); a group given by id (`sg-...`) without `--vpc` or `--instance` keeps its own VPC. The group is looked up by name in that VPC: a missing one is created (`security-group/<name>  created  sg-... vpc-...`), an existing one is used as it is (its description is kept), several are ambiguous. A group given by id that is in another VPC is exit code 1: a group cannot change VPCs. Rules present are `unchanged`; the missing ones are added in one call (ECS applies a batch as a whole). A group that is not found right after being created is retried a few times, 2 seconds apart. Rules are never removed, and without `--rule` only the group is ensured. `--instance` (name or id, found through the inventory in every selected account; several of one name are exit code 5) joins the instance to the group, keeping its other groups (`instance/<name>  updated  joined sg-... to i-...`; an instance already in it is `unchanged`); a group and an instance in different VPCs are exit code 1. `--dry-run` reads everything and reports `would create` (a new group's id is `(pending)` and all its rules would be created) and `would update  join <sg-id|(pending)> to <instance-id>`.
- `rule add <sg>` adds the rules to an existing group, like `ensure` without creating or joining anything, with `--description` on every rule added. `rule remove <sg>` removes rules given as `--rule` (every stored rule equal to it, `203.0.113.7` and `203.0.113.7/32` alike) or as `--rule-id <sgr-...>` (the ids `get` shows), not both; a rule that is not there is `unchanged`. The rules to remove are shown and confirmed (default no; `--yes` skips it, the non-interactive flow needs `--yes`, exit code 2); nothing to remove asks nothing. A rule removed meanwhile by someone else is `unchanged`.
- `delete` shows the group (name, id, VPC, rule count) and asks for a confirmation (default no; `--yes` skips it; the non-interactive flow without `--yes` is exit code 2 before the account is opened). A group that instances are still in, or that another group's rule references, is exit code 1 (`DependencyViolation`, with a hint saying which); polyctl never removes the instances or the reference for you. A group that is gone by the time it is deleted is `unchanged`.
- Errors carry the Alibaba Cloud code and message; RAM denials (`Forbidden.RAM`) are exit code 3.

ECS key pairs belong to one region (`--region`, the profile's region by default): a key used in several regions is imported in each. ECS imports RSA, ECDSA and ED25519 public keys and never returns them; it reports a fingerprint, the MD5 of the key in lower-case hexadecimal without colons, and that is what key pairs are compared by.

- `--ssh-key` takes the public key itself (one line); `--ssh-key-file` a file holding it (`~` is expanded, the first public key line is used). Alibaba Cloud accepts almost any text as a public key, so the key is checked locally: a file path given to `--ssh-key`, both flags together, a private key, text that is not a public key or a DSA key are usage errors (exit code 2) before anything is sent. The public key is never printed (reports show its algorithm, the start of its SHA256 fingerprint and of its comment), and only the key, not its comment, is sent.
- Names hold 2 to 128 characters and no `*` (which Alibaba Cloud reads as a wildcard); anything else is exit code 2.
- `key-pair list` shows `NAME`, `FINGERPRINT` and `CREATED`, every page of the region (cached in the local inventory per region). In JSON each key pair is `{"name", "fingerprint", "created", "region"}`; `created` is RFC 3339 (Alibaba Cloud keeps minutes only). No key pairs is `[]`, a note on stderr and exit code 0.
- `key-pair import <name>` imports the key and prints a change report (`key-pair/<name>  created`, with the fingerprint in `attrs`). A name that is taken is a usage error (exit code 2, as on AWS) whatever key it holds (an existing key pair is never replaced; use `ensure`). `--dry-run` checks the key and the name and reports `would create`.
- `key-pair ensure [<name>]` reuses an existing key pair first. With a public key, the key pairs holding it are looked up by fingerprint (one filtered request, then the whole listing when none matches), and one that holds it is `unchanged` (`matched by fingerprint`) under its own name. The same key can be imported under several names: `<name>` picks one of them, otherwise a terminal asks and the non-interactive flow fails with exit code 5, listing the names. When no key pair holds the key, it is imported as `<name>`; without `<name>` a terminal asks for the name, with no default, checked as it is typed (leaving the question creates nothing, exit code 0) and the non-interactive flow is a usage error (exit code 2): no name is ever chosen for you. With `<name>` alone, an existing key pair is used as it is; a missing one needs a public key (a terminal asks for the file, offering `~/.ssh/*.pub`; otherwise exit code 2). A `<name>` that exists with another key is exit code 2 and is never replaced. Without a key and without `<name>`, a terminal lists the existing key pairs (the one holding a `~/.ssh/*.pub` key marked) and "Import a new key pair from a public key file..."; the non-interactive flow is exit code 2. `--dry-run` reports `unchanged` or `would create  import from ...` without writing.
- There is no `key-pair delete`: a key pair may still be bound to instances.

`aliyun ecs image latest` prints the id of the newest public (system) image of `--os` for `--arch` (`x86_64` by default, or `arm64`) in the region (`--region`, else the profile's). It reads every page of `DescribeImages` (`ImageOwnerAlias=system`, the OS type of `--os`, the architecture, `Status=Available`, 100 a page) and keeps the images whose image family matches the pattern of the OS; the number in the family is the OS version, the highest version wins, then the newest creation time. No family name or image id is written into polyctl, so a new version is picked as soon as Alibaba Cloud publishes it. `alibaba-cloud-linux` (default) matches the standard LTS images, `acs:alibaba_cloud_linux_<version>[_<release>]_lts_x64` (`_lts_arm64` for arm64), such as `acs:alibaba_cloud_linux_4_lts_x64` or `acs:alibaba_cloud_linux_3_2104_lts_x64`: the pro, container optimized, agentic, GPU and compliance variants never match. `windows` matches Windows Server Datacenter in Chinese, `acs:win_<version>_datacenter_x64_zh` (such as `acs:win_2025_datacenter_x64_zh`): the English, Core, UEFI and container editions never match, and there is no arm64 image (exit code 2). Other systems (Ubuntu, Debian, ...) are not supported by `--os` (exit code 2): pass their image id with `--image` to `create` and `ensure`. Image names and OS names are never used to tell versions apart. The table output is the image id alone (`$(polyctl aliyun ecs image latest)`); `-o json` gives `{"image_id", "image_name", "os_name", "architecture", "creation_time", "region"}`. Image ids are regional. No matching image is exit code 4 (`no public <OS> image matching <pattern> in <region> for <arch>; pass --image <id>`). It is read-only: `--dry-run` changes nothing in it, and `--offline` is exit code 6.

VPCs and vSwitches belong to one region (`--region`, the profile's region by default) and are always read from the API (`--offline` is exit code 6; `--dry-run` changes nothing in the read-only commands). Alibaba Cloud keeps the names of neither unique: several VPCs may share a name, and vSwitches may share one within a VPC (in other zones) and across VPCs. A name held by several is ambiguous (exit code 5: pass the id, `vpc-...` / `vsw-...`), a missing one is exit code 4. Names may not start with `vpc-` / `vsw-`, `http://` or `https://`, and hold at most 128 characters.

- `vpc list` shows `NAME`, `ID`, `CIDR`, `STATUS` and `DEFAULT`, every page of the region; the default VPC (the one Alibaba Cloud creates on its own) is left out unless `--all` is given. JSON: `[{"id", "name", "cidr", "region", "is_default", "status"}]`. No VPCs is `[]`, a note on stderr and exit code 0. `vpc get` adds the VPC's vSwitches (`"vswitches"`, shaped like the rows of `vswitch list`) and the profile.
- `vpc create <name>` creates the VPC with `--cidr` (`172.16.0.0/12` by default; an IPv4 block aligned to its mask, which cannot be changed later). A VPC of that name in the region is exit code 1 (use `ensure`). `--wait` returns once it is `Available` (about 3 seconds), bounded by `--timeout` (60s).
- `vpc ensure [<name>]` makes sure the VPC (default `naming.aliyun_vpc`, `{region}-vpc`) exists and holds a vSwitch in each of `--zones`, like `vswitch ensure`; a second run changes nothing. A missing VPC is created and waited for until it is `Available` (polled by id every 2 seconds, bounded by `--timeout`, 60s by default; running out of time is exit code 1 with the last status), because a `Pending` VPC refuses vSwitches. An existing VPC is used as it is (`unchanged`; its CIDR block is not compared, a note says when `--cidr` differs), and one still `Pending` is waited for, never created again. `--no-wait` returns once the creation was requested; the vSwitches of a VPC that is not `Available` yet are then `skipped` (run `ensure` again). `--dry-run` reports `vpc/<name>  would create  172.16.0.0/12` with the id `(pending)`, and the vSwitches of a VPC still to be created with the blocks of an empty VPC (`... (vpc pending)`).
- `vswitch list` shows `NAME`, `ID`, `VPC`, `ZONE`, `CIDR`, `STATUS` and `AVAILABLE IPS`, of the region or of `--vpc` (id or name). JSON: `[{"id", "name", "vpc_id", "zone", "cidr", "status", "available_ips", "region"}]`. No vSwitches is `[]` and exit code 0. `vswitch get` looks a name up in `--vpc` when given, else in the whole region.
- `vswitch create <name>` needs `--vpc` and `--zone` (a suffix such as `b`, or a zone id of the region such as `cn-hongkong-b`; nothing is sent without them, exit code 2). `--cidr` gives its block; without it the first free `/22` of the VPC's block is taken. A vSwitch of that name in the same zone of the VPC is exit code 1 (use `ensure`).
- `vswitch ensure` makes sure the VPC `--vpc` (default `naming.aliyun_vpc`, which has to exist: otherwise exit code 2 naming it) holds a vSwitch in each of `--zones` (`b,c` by default: `{region}-b` and `{region}-c`; suffixes or zone ids of the region, each once). They are named `<prefix>-<suffix>` with `--prefix` (no extra hyphen when the prefix ends with one), else by `naming.aliyun_vswitch` (`{region}-switch-{zone}`, `{zone}` being the suffix), and looked up by name in that VPC only: a vSwitch of the name in another VPC never counts. An existing one is `unchanged` whatever its zone or block (a warning in the detail says when its zone differs; vSwitches cannot be changed). The missing ones get the first free `/--netmask` blocks (22 by default, 16 to 29) of the VPC's block, scanned from its start and never overlapping the VPC's other vSwitches, so `--dry-run` shows the blocks a real run creates (`vswitch/<name>  created  vsw-... <zone> <cidr>`). A new vSwitch is waited for up to 30 seconds; running out of time is only a warning.
- vSwitch creation errors carry the Alibaba Cloud code with a hint: an overlapping block (`InvalidCidrBlock.Overlapped`), a block outside the VPC's (`InvalidParameter`) or not aligned (`IllegalParam.CidrBlock`) are exit code 1; a zone the region does not have (`ResourceNotAvailable`) is exit code 7, with a hint to pass `--zones`.

Elastic IP addresses (EIPs) belong to one region (`--region`, the profile's region by default) and are named by their allocation id (`eip-...`) or their address. An EIP is billed as long as it exists, bound or not; polyctl has no command that releases one (`list --unused` shows the idle ones).

- New EIPs are allocated with `AllocateEipAddress`: `--bandwidth` Mbps (`defaults.aliyun.eip.bandwidth`, else 200), `--isp` (`defaults.aliyun.eip.isp`, else `BGP_PRO`), pay by traffic, pay as you go, public, paid automatically, in the resource group `--resource-group` (a name, looked up with ResourceManager, or an `rg-...` id taken as it is; default `defaults.aliyun.resource_group`, else the group named `default`). A resource group that does not exist is exit code 4. An ISP the region does not offer is exit code 7 (which code Alibaba Cloud answers with is not verified: an invalid component, or a code naming the ISP, is mapped to it). An allocated EIP is read back (the answer of the allocation lacks most fields) and waited for until it is `Available` (up to 30 seconds).
- `list` shows `ALLOCATION_ID`, `IP`, `STATUS`, `BOUND_TO` (the instance, network interface or load balancer and its type), `NAME / DESC`, `BANDWIDTH` and `ISP`, every page of the region, cached in the local inventory per region. `--unused` keeps the EIPs bound to nothing, `--bound` the others (both together are exit code 2). JSON: `[{"allocation_id", "ip", "status", "instance_id", "instance_type", "name", "description", "bandwidth", "isp", "charge_type", "internet_charge_type", "service_managed", "resource_group_id", "region"}]`. No EIPs is `[]`, a note on stderr and exit code 0.
- `allocate` allocates one EIP and prints a change report (`eip/1  created  allocated 203.0.113.20 BGP_PRO 200Mbps PayByTraffic`); `--dry-run` resolves the resource group and reports `would create`.
- `associate <allocation-id|ip> --instance <name|id>` binds the EIP to an ECS instance (found through the inventory in every selected account; `--instance` is required). The EIP is looked up in the instance's region: a `--region` that differs from it is exit code 2, a missing EIP exit code 4. An EIP bound to the instance already is `unchanged`; an EIP bound elsewhere or not `Available`, an instance that holds an EIP already or is not in a VPC are exit code 1. After the binding the EIP is waited for until it is `InUse` by the instance (up to 30 seconds).
- `ensure` makes sure `--count` EIPs (1 by default; 2 for a dual-zone load balancer) are there to use, and with `--instance` that the instance holds one; a second run changes nothing. An idle EIP of the account in the region is reused first (`eip/1  unchanged  reuse idle 203.0.113.10`): bound to nothing, `Available`, not managed by a cloud product (`ServiceManaged` 0: a load balancer keeps the EIPs it used managed for up to a minute after letting them go) and of the ISP asked for. Any idle EIP of the account qualifies, whoever allocated it. The EIPs picked by one run are never picked twice, and `--exclude` leaves allocation ids out. The rest are allocated (`created`). `--instance` (with `--count 1` only) binds the EIP to the instance (`instance/<name>  updated  associated eip-... with i-...`); an instance that holds an EIP already is `unchanged` and nothing is reused, allocated or bound. `-o json` adds the EIPs to the report: `"result": {"eips": [{"allocation_id", "ip"}]}` (`(pending)` for one a dry run would allocate). `--dry-run` reads everything, so its `reuse idle` rows are those a real run reports.

Application Load Balancer (ALB) load balancers, listeners, server groups and access control lists belong to one region (`--region`, the profile's region by default) and are always read from the API (`--offline` is exit code 6), except `load-balancer list`, which reads the local inventory (type `load_balancer`, every region of the account). Alibaba Cloud keeps none of their names unique: a name held by several is ambiguous (exit code 5: pass the id, `alb-...`, `sgp-...`, `acl-...`); a missing one is exit code 4. ALB's own "not found" answers (HTTP 404 `ResourceNotFound.*`) are exit code 4; a listener or ACL still changing (`IncorrectStatus.*`) and a full ACL (`ResourceQuotaExceeded.*`, whose message quotes the account's real limit) are exit code 1 with a hint. A RAM user without ALB permissions is exit code 3, except in `sync` and the inventory lookups, where the load balancers of that account are a warning and an empty list.

- `load-balancer list` shows `ID`, `NAME`, `STATUS`, `VPC`, `REGION` and `DNS_NAME`; `--region` keeps one region, `--name` the names that contain the text. No load balancer is `[]`, a note on stderr and exit code 0. `load-balancer get <name|id>` finds the load balancer through the inventory of every selected account (synced once when it does not know the name; several of the name are exit code 5, and `--offline` without a match exit code 6), or asks `--region` of the selected profile directly. JSON: `{"id", "name", "status", "region", "vpc_id", "dns_name", "address_type", "edition", "resource_group_id", "deletion_protection", "addresses": [{"zone", "vswitch_id", "allocation_id", "ip"}], "profile", "listeners": [{"id", "protocol", "port", "status", "certificate_id"}]}`; the addresses are sorted by zone (ALB returns its zones in no particular order).
- `load-balancer create <name>` creates an Internet-facing IPv4 Standard load balancer, pay as you go, on the EIPs given (`Fixed`), in `--vpc` (an id) with exactly two `--zone-mapping <zone-id>:<vswitch-id>:<eip-allocation-id>`; it creates nothing it depends on (use `ensure`). The EIPs have to share one ISP. A load balancer of the name in the region is exit code 1. `--wait` waits until it is `Active` (about 45 seconds), bounded by `--timeout`.
- `load-balancer update <name|id>` (found like `get`) changes what the flags name, each only when it differs (otherwise the row is `unchanged`; no flag at all is exit code 2): `--name` renames the load balancer (a name another load balancer of the region holds is exit code 1), `--deletion-protection[=false]` switches its deletion protection. Rows: `load-balancer/<name>/deletion-protection` (`false -> true`) and `load-balancer/<name>/name` (`<old> -> <new>`); `--dry-run` reports them as `would update`. After a rename the command waits until the load balancer is `Active` again, bounded by `--timeout` (`--no-wait` returns at once). It asks for no confirmation.
- `load-balancer delete <name|id>` shows the load balancer, its addresses and listeners, and asks for a confirmation (default no; `--yes` skips it, the non-interactive flow needs `--yes`, exit code 2 before the account is opened). ALB deletes the listeners with the load balancer, so one that still has listeners needs `--force` (exit code 2 without it). Deletion protection is exit code 1 with the `load-balancer update <id> --deletion-protection=false` command to run; delete never switches it off. Server groups are kept (a deleted load balancer's listeners linger for about a minute and keep them in use). The EIPs are unbound, not released: they stay billed, and a note names each. `--wait` waits until the load balancer is gone.
- `load-balancer ensure <domain> --service <ecs-name|id>` makes sure a dual-zone load balancer with HTTP and HTTPS listeners serves the ECS instance; a second run changes nothing. Before anything is written the instance (in the account and region of the command; another account or region is exit code 4) and, when an HTTPS listener is wanted, the certificate are looked up: a missing one is exit code 4 with nothing created. The load balancer is named by `naming.aliyun_alb_load_balancer` (`lb-{name}`, `{name}` being the domain with its dots replaced by hyphens; a wildcard domain is exit code 2) and looked up by name. An existing one is used as it is (its VPC, zones and EIPs are not touched; one in another VPC than the instance is exit code 1); one that is not `Active` yet is waited for, an `Inactive` one is exit code 1. A missing one is created in the instance's VPC (`--vpc` may name it, nothing else) in two zones (`--zones`, else the first two zones of the region offering ALB, `DescribeZones`; fewer than two is exit code 7), each with the vSwitch `naming.aliyun_vswitch` names in that VPC (a missing one is created on the first free `/22`, like `vswitch ensure`) and an EIP: an idle EIP of the account of the ISP `defaults.aliyun.eip.isp` whose name and description are empty is reused (`eip/1  updated  reuse eip-... <ip>`), else one is allocated like `eip address allocate`. It is Standard, pay as you go, in the resource group `defaults.aliyun.resource_group` (else the group named `default`; a missing group is exit code 4), and is waited for until `Active` (every 5 seconds, bounded by `--timeout`); `--no-wait` skips the wait and every later step. The server group `naming.aliyun_alb_server_group` (`{service}-http`, or `--server-group`) of that VPC is created when missing (type `Instance`, health check and session persistence off) and gets the instance on port 80 when it has no server; a group with servers is left as it is. With `--https-backend` the HTTPS listener forwards to a group of its own (`{service}-https`, or `--server-group` without a trailing `-http` plus `-https`; protocol HTTPS, port 443), otherwise it reuses the HTTP group (`server-group/{service}-https  skipped`). The listeners (`--listener <protocol>:<port>`, http or https, `http:80` and `https:443` by default) that are missing are created and waited for until `Running` (up to a minute). A listener matches by protocol and port; `http:80` and `https:443` otherwise take an existing listener of their protocol whatever its port (one no other `--listener` matched). Of an existing listener only the default certificate of an HTTPS one is compared; another one is replaced and the listener waited for until `Running` again. The certificate is `--cert` (a CAS certificate id), else the latest valid certificate uploaded for the domain's registered domain (named `upload_<root>_<date>`, else whose common name is the root domain, its wildcard or the domain), from the CAS store of the region; ensure never issues one (run `cert ensure` and `aliyun cas certificate upload` first). Nothing created is rolled back when a later step fails: run `ensure` again to go on. DNS is not written: the last row, `dns/addresses  skipped`, and a note on stderr in a table name the addresses to point the domain to (`polyctl dns ensure`). Rows: `vpc/<name>`, `vswitch/<name>`, `eip/1`, `eip/2`, `load-balancer/<name>`, `server-group/<name>`, `server-group/<name>/server`, `load-balancer/<name>/listener/<proto>:<port>`, `dns/addresses`. `-o json` adds `"load_balancer": {"id", "name", "dns_name"}` and `"addresses"` (sorted by zone; empty when a dry run would create the load balancer). `--dry-run` reads everything and reports what a real run would do, with `(pending)` for ids that do not exist yet. `--eips` takes 2 only.
- Listener certificates are CAS certificate ids written as the region needs them: `<id>-ap-southeast-1` in `cn-hongkong` (its certificates live in the Singapore CAS store; the bare id is refused there), the bare id elsewhere (not verified outside `cn-hongkong`); `defaults.aliyun.cas_region` overrides the store. `--cert` takes the integer id, or an id already written with its suffix.
- `listener list --lb <name|id>` (required) shows `ID`, `PROTOCOL`, `PORT`, `STATUS` and `SERVER_GROUPS`; `listener get <listener-id>` adds the certificates (JSON `{"id", "load_balancer_id", "protocol", "port", "status", "default_action", "server_groups", "certificates", "region"}`). `listener create` creates a listener on `--lb` for `--listener <proto:port>` forwarding to `--server-group`; an HTTPS one needs `--cert`, an HTTP one takes none, a port the load balancer listens on already is exit code 1. `listener update <listener-id>` changes `--server-group` and/or `--cert` and nothing else (values held already are `unchanged`). `listener cert set <listener-id> --cert <id>` makes the certificate the default certificate of an HTTPS listener (`unchanged` when it is already; `--dry-run` reports `listener/<id>  would update  <id> cert <old> -> <new>`). Every listener write waits up to a minute for the listener to be `Running` again (a listener still `Provisioning` or `Configuring` refuses the next write). `listener delete <listener-id>` asks first.
- `server-group list [--name]`, `server-group get <name|id>` (with its servers: `{"server_id", "type", "port", "ip", "weight", "status"}`), `server-group create <name> --vpc --protocol HTTP|HTTPS` (type `Instance`, health check and session persistence off; a group of the name in the VPC is exit code 1), `server-group delete` (asks first; a group a listener forwards to is refused by Alibaba Cloud, exit code 1) and `server-group server add <group> --instance <ecs> --port <n>` (an instance of the group's account and region; one already in the group on that port is `unchanged`; an instance of another VPC is exit code 1).
- `server-group update <name|id>` changes what the flags name, in one row `server-group/<name>` (`name web -> web-2; scheduler Wrr -> Wlc`; `unchanged` when nothing differs, no flag at all is exit code 2): `--name` renames the group (a name another group of its VPC holds is exit code 1), `--scheduler` sets the balancing algorithm (`Wrr`, `Wlc` or `Sch`, any case), `--health-check[=false]` switches the health check on (the API's defaults: HTTP, path `/`) or off, `--health-check-path` sets the path of an HTTP or HTTPS health check (it has to start with `/`; the health check has to be on, or switched on by `--health-check`, else exit code 2), `--sticky-session[=false]` switches session persistence on (the load balancer inserts the cookie) or off. Other health check settings, the cookie of a `Server` session persistence, slow start and connection draining are left to the console. After a change the command waits up to a minute for the group to be `Available` again. It asks for no confirmation. `server-group get` shows the scheduler and session persistence too (JSON adds `"health_check_protocol"`, `"health_check_path"` (when the health check is on), `"scheduler"` and `"sticky_session"`).
- `acl list [--name]`, `acl get <name|id>` (JSON `{"id", "name", "status", "resource_group_id", "region", "entries": [{"entry", "description", "status"}], "entry_count"}`), `acl create <name>` (a name taken already is exit code 1), `acl delete` (asks first; the entries go with it), `acl entry add <acl> <cidr>...` and `acl entry remove <acl> <cidr>...`. Entries are IPv4 addresses or CIDR blocks: a bare address is written as `/32` (ALB refuses it without a mask), a block with host bits set and IPv6 are exit code 2. Entries the ACL holds already are `unchanged` (ALB refuses a whole batch that repeats one), missing ones are `unchanged` for remove; the others are written 20 per call (the API limit), the ACL waited for until `Available` after each. An empty `--description` is not sent. An ACL takes effect once bound to a listener, which polyctl does not do.

Certificate Management Service (CAS) holds the certificates uploaded to the account, which ALB listeners reference by id. An account has two separate certificate stores, `cas.aliyuncs.com` and `cas.ap-southeast-1.aliyuncs.com`: a certificate uploaded to one is missing from the other (its id is not found there). polyctl uses the Singapore store for a profile whose region is `cn-hongkong` (the ALB listeners of that region take the id as `<id>-ap-southeast-1`) and the central store otherwise; `defaults.aliyun.cas_region` and `defaults.aliyun.cas_endpoint` override the region and the endpoint. An endpoint that refuses every call (`API.Forbidden`, as `cas.cn-hongkong.aliyuncs.com` does) is exit code 7. Only uploaded certificates (`OrderType` `UPLOAD`) are handled, never the free or bought certificate orders. A RAM user without the `yundun-cert:*` permissions is exit code 3.

- `certificate list` shows `ID`, `NAME`, `COMMON_NAME`, `START`, `END` and `DAYS`, every page, cached in the local inventory (type `certificate`). The dates are the UTC dates CAS reports; `DAYS` is the whole days from today (UTC) to the end date. A certificate counts as expired from 00:00 UTC of its end date on, and so does one without an end date. `--expired` keeps the expired ones, `--expires-within <days>` those with at most that many days left (expired ones included), `--name` those whose name holds the text. JSON: `[{"id", "name", "common_name", "start_date", "end_date", "days_to_expire", "expired", "fingerprint_sha1", "sans"}]`; `fingerprint_sha1` is the SHA-1 of the certificate as CAS writes it (upper-case hexadecimal without colons, as `openssl x509 -fingerprint -sha1` without the colons). No certificate is `[]`, a note on stderr and exit code 0.
- `certificate upload <name>` uploads `--cert` (the PEM chain, leaf first) with its unencrypted PEM private key `--key`; `-` reads one of them from stdin. The files are checked before anything is sent: unreadable PEM, an encrypted key, a key that does not belong to the first certificate and an expired certificate are exit code 2. Names are unique within a store and certificates are told apart by name only: when an uploaded certificate has the name already it is reused as it is (`unchanged`, with its id), whatever it holds. Naming certificates `upload_<root domain>_<YYYY-MM-DD>` after their expiry (the date of `NotAfter`, UTC) makes a renewed certificate a new upload and the same certificate a reuse; CAS accepts the same certificate under another name. The upload is synchronous. Report: `cas/certificate/<name>  created  <id>  end YYYY-MM-DD`; `--dry-run` checks the files and the names and reports `would create` with the id `(pending)`.
- `certificate delete <cert-id>` deletes an uploaded certificate (a missing id is exit code 4); `--expired`, without an id, deletes every expired one. CAS deletes a certificate a listener still uses without a word and leaves the listener with a dangling id, so polyctl first reads the HTTPS and QUIC listeners of the account's ALB load balancers, in every region of the account or in the `--region` ones (read-only, also in a dry run), and refuses to delete a certificate one of them holds (as its default, an additional or a CA certificate): exit code 1, naming each listener (`cannot delete certificate <id>: used by listener <listener-id> of load balancer <name> (<region>)`). With `--expired` such a certificate is `skipped` and the others are deleted; a deletion that fails is reported, the others go on, and the command ends with exit code 1. When the listeners of a region cannot be read nothing is deleted (exit code 3 for a permission denial, 1 otherwise, naming the region); a region of the account without an ALB endpoint has no listeners, while a `--region` one must be readable. Only ALB listeners are checked, which a note on stderr says: classic load balancers, API gateways, WAF and other products are not. `--force` deletes without the check. A confirmation is asked first (default no; `--yes` skips it, and the non-interactive flow needs `--yes`, also with `--force`). `--dry-run` reports `would delete`, or `skipped` (`would refuse: used by ...`) for a certificate in use, and asks nothing.

Inventory types: `zone` (Alidns domains), `dns_record`, `domain` (registered domains), `instance`, `security_group`, `key_pair`, `eip`, `load_balancer` (ALB), `certificate` (uploaded CAS certificates). Alidns stores the relative record name as `RR`; both the native `aliyun dns record` commands and the neutral `dns` commands expose it as `--name`. Records also carry a resolution line (`--line`), shown in the `LINE` column.

## Output formats

```sh
polyctl dns list example.com                 # table (default)
polyctl dns list example.com -o json         # pretty-printed JSON
polyctl dns list example.com -o yaml
POLYCTL_OUTPUT=json polyctl auth list        # from the environment
```

Precedence: `--output` > `POLYCTL_OUTPUT` > `output:` in `config.yaml` > `table`: flag, environment, config file, default, as for every setting.

Tables are tab-aligned and contain only the most useful columns. JSON and YAML encode the full result, including routing information, for example:

```json
{
  "target": { "platform": "cloudflare", "profile": "acme", "resource_type": "zone", "resource_id": "9a1...", "domain": "example.com" },
  "cached": true,
  "records": [
    { "id": "f3c...", "zone_id": "9a1...", "name": "www.example.com", "type": "A", "value": "203.0.113.10", "ttl": 1, "proxied": true }
  ]
}
```

Human-readable progress and notes always go to stderr, so stdout is safe to pipe.

### Dry runs and change reports

`ensure` commands, and every write command run with `--dry-run`, print a change report: one row per resource, with what happened to it (or would happen).

Most write commands print the change report when they run for real too. The exceptions are the older single-object writes, which keep their 0.1 output so that scripts reading it go on working: when they run for real they print the object they wrote, and only `--dry-run` prints a change report. They are:

- `dns add`, `dns set`, `dns delete` (the records) and `domain ns set` (the domain);
- the DNS record writes of the platform trees: `cloudflare dns create|update|delete`, `namecom dns create|update|delete`, `godaddy dns add|upsert|delete`, `aws route53 record upsert|delete`, `gcp dns record upsert|delete`, `aliyun dns record create|update|delete`;
- the registrar writes: `ns set` of `aws route53domains`, `namecom domain`, `godaddy domain` and `aliyun domain`, and `lock` / `unlock` / `autorenew` of `namecom domain` and `godaddy domain`;
- `cloudflare cache purge`, `cloudflare dev-mode` / `ssl-mode` with a value, and `aws rds instance create` (the instance).

A script that reads the output of one of these should read the object, not a report.

```text
RESOURCE                    ACTION        DETAIL
security-group/web          unchanged     sg-0abc
security-group/web/rule     created       tcp 443 0.0.0.0/0
record/www.example.com/A    would update  203.0.113.10 -> 203.0.113.20
```

The actions are `created`, `updated`, `unchanged`, `skipped`, `deleted` and `failed`, and in a dry run `would create`, `would update` and `would delete` (`would_create`, `would_update`, `would_delete` in JSON and YAML). With `-o json` / `-o yaml` the report is

```json
{
  "target": { "platform": "aws", "profile": "acme", "region": "ap-east-1" },
  "dry_run": false,
  "changes": [
    { "resource": "security-group/web", "action": "unchanged", "id": "sg-0abc" },
    { "resource": "security-group/web/rule", "action": "created", "detail": "tcp 443 0.0.0.0/0" }
  ]
}
```

A report with nothing in it still prints its header (`"changes": []`). When a step fails, the report is printed all the same, with the failed step as `failed` and the error as its detail, and the command exits with the code of the error. Commands that work through a batch (several records, several accounts) go on after a failed item, report every item and exit with code 1 at the end when any failed.

## Exit codes

| Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `0` | Success, including an `ensure` that found everything unchanged and any `--dry-run`. Also every status `domain ns check` reports (`mismatch`, `no-zone`, ... included): it is a report, not an assertion. Also a menu or selection prompt left with Esc or Ctrl-C, a question of a login flow left with Ctrl-C or Ctrl-D, and a command that had nothing to do: no account to `sync` or `domain index` (none configured, none matching `--profile` / `--platform`, none supporting `--type`), no profile or account to pick from in `auth use` / `auth logout` / `auth manage`, a declined or left confirmation of any command of [Confirmations](#confirmations) (the deletion of expired certificates in `cert deploy --prune-expired` included), and the terms of service of the CA declined in `cert ensure`. Also a `cert deploy` that found no certificate target in the selected accounts. The prompts in this row exist in the interactive flow only. |
| `1` | Generic error (API failure, I/O error, `find --refresh` that could sync no account and found nothing, a login that was not approved in time, `config.yaml` changed by another process while the command ran, a failed certificate issuance or a certificate lock that did not come free, a `cert deploy` target that failed, ...). |
| `2` | Usage error: bad flag combination, unknown flag or command (also below a command group: `polyctl dns nosuch`), unknown platform or resource type, missing required flag or argument, an unknown value of a login option (`--set env=`, `--set method=`), an invalid or already used name for `auth rename`, invalid config, an invalid `POLYCTL_NON_INTERACTIVE` value, `auth login` without `--profile` for a platform that only other profiles have. Also everything the non-interactive flow cannot ask for: `auth manage`, a missing argument (`auth login` without a platform, `auth use` without a profile, `auth logout` without `--profile`), a confirmation without `--yes` (every command of [Confirmations](#confirmations)), and a login value or choice that was not passed (the message names the `--set` key or environment variable). Also `cert ensure` (and `cert deploy` when it issues) without an ACME account address or the agreement to the terms of service when it has to register an account, and `cert deploy` with both a domain and `--all` or neither, an unknown `--deployer`, or a `cert.skip_accounts` entry naming an unknown deployer. |
| `3` | Authentication: missing credentials, no account on the platform in the selected profile, rejected token, expired SSO session, `auth status` with a failing account. Also a request valid credentials were not allowed to make: a RAM / IAM policy denial (the platform's error code is kept in the message), or an account the platform refuses to serve until something is done in its console (Alibaba Cloud basic information not completed, real-name verification missing). Such a denial does not ask for a new login, and `sync` goes on with the account's other resource types. Also a `cert deploy` whose every failure was one of these. |
| `4` | Not found: unknown domain / zone / record / profile, a `--profile` / `--platform` selection that names no account able to hold the requested domain (`dns list`, `domain info`, ...), `auth logout <platform>` for a platform the profile has no account on, a set that was never synced in `--offline` mode, a domain no DNS account hosts in `cert ensure`, a domain without a certificate in `cert get`, or a domain `cert deploy` finds neither in the local store nor in a DNS account. Also `find` without a match, or with an inventory that never held the sets it searches. |
| `5` | Ambiguous: the domain has more than one candidate account (pass `--profile` and/or `--platform`, as the message says), or the platform more than one candidate profile (pass `--profile`). Also a `dns ensure` record whose name and type has several records, none of them as desired (pass `--prune-duplicates`). |
| `6` | Offline: the command needs the network but `--offline` was given. |
| `7` | Not supported: the platform (or the profile's client) cannot perform the operation. Also `cert deploy --platform` naming a platform without a certificate deployer. |

A non-zero code means the request could not be carried out. Having nothing to do is success: the command exits with code 0 and says why on stderr as a note, `nothing to do: <reason>`, without the `error:` prefix, and a command that prints a report still prints it, empty (`[]` with `-o json`), so scripts can test the exit code and parse stdout either way.

Errors are printed to stderr as `error: <message>`.

## Project layout

```text
cmd/polyctl/                 main(): calls internal/cli.Main and exits with its code
internal/cli/                Cobra command tree (root, auth, dns, domain, cert, cert deploy, find, sync/inventory,
                             platform mounts), exit-code mapping
internal/app/                Runtime shared by all commands: config, credential store, inventory, profile selection,
                             domain routing (ResolveDomain), resource routing (ResolveResource), cache policy
                             (EnsureSet / NeedsRefresh), SyncAccount, --dry-run and change reports, --wait flags,
                             per-platform defaults and naming, hooks.on_failure, the NS check of domain ns check,
                             the search of find
internal/platform/           Provider-neutral contracts: Provider, Client, DNSService, RegistrarService, Zone/Record/Domain,
                             resource types, sentinel errors, provider registry, DNS name helpers, live NS lookups and
                             the provider guess; the change plan of ensure commands (Plan / Step / Change / Report),
                             waits and retries, the shared parsers of ingress rules, listeners and DNS record specs,
                             the planner of dns ensure, SSH public keys and key pair resolution, the load balancer
                             contract (LoadBalancerService) and its target parser, the certificate deployer contract
                             and registry of cert deploy
internal/providers/
  cloudflare/  namecom/  godaddy/  aws/  gcp/  aliyun/
                             One package per platform: provider.go (login, client, sync), commands.go (the command
                             tree and the older native commands), and a client file and a command file per service
                             or resource (aws: ec2.go, instance.go / instancecmd.go, elbv2*.go, rds*.go, acm*.go, ...),
                             plus certdeploy.go (its cert deploy targets) and find.go (its part of find) where it has them
internal/cert/               The certificate store, the ACME account and issuance (lego), the DNS-01 challenge records
                             written through a DNSService, the propagation check, the cert ensure flow and the
                             cert deploy flow
internal/config/             config.yaml loading/saving (profiles and their accounts), TTL defaults, defaults / naming /
                             hooks / cert / dns settings
internal/creds/              Keyring store, 0600 file store, Multi (read from any, write to primary)
internal/secrets/            Instance login credentials sealed with AES-256-GCM in inventory.db (data key in the
                             credential store), the Windows password generator
internal/inventory/          SQLite schema, Replace/Upsert/Delete, Purge/RenameProfile, domain lookup, IP index, stats
internal/output/             table / json / yaml rendering
internal/dnstest/            Fake DNS resolvers on 127.0.0.1 for tests
internal/httpx/              Small JSON REST client with retries (Cloudflare, name.com, GoDaddy)
internal/prompt/             Line / secret / confirm prompts in the flow handed to them (they refuse in the
                             non-interactive one); Picker (arrow-key select, multi-select, confirm, input) drawn on stderr
internal/browser/            Open a URL in the default browser (macOS, Windows, Linux)
internal/loopback/           The 127.0.0.1 listener that takes the redirect of a browser login (GCP, Cloudflare)
```

Adding a platform means implementing `platform.Provider` (and `DNSService` / `RegistrarService` as applicable) in a new `internal/providers/<name>` package, registering it in `init()`, and mounting its Cobra subtree in `internal/cli/platforms.go`. The neutral commands, routing, caching and output need no changes.

## Security notes

- Secrets never enter `config.yaml`, the inventory or command output. `auth list` shows metadata only. The one exception is the login of a Windows instance polyctl created: stored encrypted in the inventory, and printed only by an interactive create and by the `password` command of the instance group.
- Keyring entries are created under the service name `polyctl`; on macOS you may be asked once to allow `polyctl` access to the Keychain item.
- When the keyring is unavailable, `credentials.json` is written with mode `0600` inside a `0700` directory. Treat that file like `~/.aws/credentials`. Set `credential_store: keyring` if you would rather fail than fall back.
- `inventory.db` contains the full record data of every zone you synced (including TXT verification strings and the public IPs of instances). It is not encrypted, except the Windows passwords of the `instance_secrets` table, which are sealed with a data key kept in the credential store (see Instance login credentials); keep the config directory private or point `POLYCTL_INVENTORY_DB` at a protected location.
- Secrets are read from the terminal without echo. Passing them on the command line with `--set token=...` is convenient for scripts but leaves them in shell history and in the process list; prefer the interactive prompt, or `--set key=-` with the value piped on stdin by a secrets manager.
- AWS SSO role credentials and GCP OAuth access tokens are short-lived and minted on demand; polyctl stores the Identity Center access/refresh tokens (plus the registered OIDC client) and the Google refresh token, and refreshes them when possible.
- A Cloudflare browser login (`method=oauth`) stores the OAuth access token, the refresh token, the expiry and the granted scopes in the credential store, never in `config.yaml` (which records the method, the account and the list of granted scopes) and never in output, `-v` included. The client is a public one without a secret: each login is bound to a fresh PKCE verifier and a random `state`, the redirect is taken by a listener on `127.0.0.1` only, on a random port, for one request on one path, and the page it shows contains nothing of the request. The authorization covers a whole account, so prefer an API token where single zones are enough. `auth logout` revokes the refresh token and the access token at Cloudflare, best effort: when Cloudflare cannot be reached, or with `--offline`, a note says that the authorization could not be revoked, the account is removed all the same, and the authorization stays valid until you withdraw it in the Cloudflare dashboard.
- Create least-privilege tokens: Cloudflare tokens scoped to the zones you manage, GoDaddy/name.com tokens per machine, AWS roles limited to Route 53 / EC2, GCP service accounts with the DNS Administrator and Compute Instance Admin roles only.
- Network access goes directly to each platform's public API over HTTPS with a `polyctl/<version>` User-Agent; there is no telemetry and no intermediary service.

## Roadmap

- Cloud resources beyond DNS cover the common operations, not each cloud's whole surface: for Alibaba Cloud the ECS instance lifecycle (list, get, create, delete, reboot, resize, start, stop, ensure), ECS security groups, key pairs and the latest system image (`aliyun ecs`), VPCs and vSwitches (`aliyun vpc`), elastic IP addresses (`aliyun eip`), ALB load balancers, listeners, server groups and ACLs (including `load-balancer ensure`, `aliyun alb`) and the certificates of Certificate Management Service (`aliyun cas`); AWS has the EC2 instance lifecycle (list, get, create, delete, reboot, resize, start, stop), EC2 key pairs, security groups, EBS volumes and snapshots, ELBv2 load balancers, target groups and listeners (including `load-balancer ensure`), ACM certificates (list, get, request, import), and RDS database instances (list, get, create for MySQL, start, stop, resize, delete, ensure); GCP has the Compute Engine instance lifecycle (list, get, create, delete, resize, reboot, update, start, stop), VPC firewall rules (including the load balancer health check rule), read-only VPC networks and subnetworks, SSL certificates, regional external load balancers (`load-balancer ensure`, `list`, `get`, `delete`, `cert set`) and their components (backend services, forwarding rules, health checks, instance groups). Across them, `cert deploy` renews and deploys the certificates of the local store to ALB listeners, GCP HTTPS proxies and ACM imported certificates; Tencent Cloud and other CDN platforms have no deployer yet. Object storage, IAM, Alibaba Cloud CDN / ESA and the rest of each cloud's surface are deliberately out of scope for now.
- GoDaddy and name.com registrar features beyond nameservers, lock, auto-renew and availability (transfers, contacts, purchases) are not planned for the first releases.
- Cloudflare coverage is limited to zones, DNS, cache purge, cache rules, redirect rules, development mode, SSL mode and the custom domains of R2 buckets; Workers, Pages, Access and the contents of R2 buckets are not exposed.
- Planned: record import/export (zone-file and bulk JSON), `polyctl domain expiring` across registrars, encrypted file fallback for the credential store, and a `--watch` mode for `inventory stats`.
